Crushing the Serpent Under the HeelPage built 13 November 2026, 07:56 Israel time

Crushing the Serpent Under the Heel

Updated 2026-11-13 05:00 Israel time. Each item says who said it, where, when, and what comes with it. Nothing here is graded. Where sources differ, the item says "another source says" and the judgment is yours.

Summary

The push to crush the gangs reached downtown Port-au-Prince on 12 Nov. The police said they and the Task Force took "total control" of the Sylvio Cator stadium, held by gangs since February 2024, in an early-morning operation and handed it to Salvadoran troops, who are turning it into a base. Le Nouvelliste counted more than twenty armored vehicles and trucks inside. No toll and no account of resistance were given. Haiti Defense Observer says Haitian soldiers had been inside since 10 Nov.

Bukele, writing in Creole, called it a joint operation of the police, the army, the Task Force and Salvadoran special forces, said "several human skulls" were found inside, and told gang members to leave "before the offensive begins." Jake Johnston says the video shows robots strapped with rifles and calls Haiti "a security lab for foreign nations." Haitian outlets say reconnaissance is under way toward Village de Dieu, the stronghold of Izo a few blocks away, and Martissant, with Carrefour next. A commentator posted coordinates of an allied gang's hideout nearby.

The Gang Suppression Force reported an early-morning raid in Cité Soleil: one suspected gang member killed, two wounded, weapons and Molotov cocktails seized. The prime minister visited the forward base at Téléco in Kenscoff with EU and OAS officials and restated the aim of retaking the territory.

Out in the Cul-de-Sac plain, Port-au-Prince Post says two sources tell it the gangs still hold the ground after the strike on Lanmò San Jou's house; a Salvadoran account says he told a live broadcast he left only to protect his mother. Jacqueline Charles of the Miami Herald says sources tell her the police had the house's location as recently as summer 2025; Johnston asks why the government acts now.

The argument over Washington's role goes on. RNDDH's Pierre Espérance says the United States wants coordination, not a halt, and calls reports otherwise disinformation that protects the gangs; AlterPresse reports organizations denouncing American pressure; left-wing networks want Bukele out. The Global Centre for the Responsibility to Protect warns about heavy weapons and drones in crowded neighborhoods and asks for clear lines of command. Supporters plan marches for Bukele on 18 Nov. The four Salvadoran airmen are still missing.

Map: Village de Dieu (18.538°N, 72.350°W) (neighborhood, approximate point) · Port-au-Prince (18.547°N, 72.340°W) (city center) · Cité Soleil (18.578°N, 72.335°W) · Martissant (18.527°N, 72.357°W) (neighborhood, approximate point) · Carrefour (18.535°N, 72.409°W) · Kenscoff (18.450°N, 72.287°W)

2026-11-13 04:50
Haitian National Police (@pnh_officiel), communiqué from its communication office (DICOP) as reported by Vant Bèf Info, Gazette Haiti and EFE; Le Nouvelliste, citing a source close to the Armed Forces of Haiti · Sylvio Cator stadium, downtown Port-au-Prince (Portail Léogâne); operation early on 12 Nov; police posts on X, Creole, 12 Nov at 8:35 p.m. and 9:05 p.m. Israel time

"PNH ak TASK FORCE gen kontwòl total estad Sylvio Cator" (the police and the Task Force have total control of the Sylvio Cator stadium). The police communiqué, as reported by Vant Bèf Info and Gazette Haiti, says the operation took place early on Thursday 12 Nov, that the surrounding areas were also retaken, and that control was then handed to the Salvadoran forces, who will "consolidate the positions." EFE (via Infobae) quotes it as putting the Salvadorans "in charge of guaranteeing security and consolidating the recovered space." The stadium, home ground of the national football team, had been held by gangs since February 2024. Le Nouvelliste says more than twenty armored vehicles and trucks carrying Salvadoran soldiers are parked inside. Gazette Haiti says it gives the Salvadorans a foothold toward Carrefour-Feuilles, Boulevard Jean-Jacques Dessalines and the southern exit of the capital on National Road 2.

Evidence: Official police posts with 4 photos and a written communiqué; Le Nouvelliste cites one unnamed source close to the army. Vant Bèf Info and Gazette Haiti note the police gave no toll, no account of resistance and no next steps. · open the original
Another source says: Another source says on timing: Haiti Defense Observer (@HT_DefenseObsvr) says the army was already inside the stadium on Monday or Tuesday, 9 or 10 Nov, and posted a video it dates to 10 Nov. Vant Bèf Info, Radio Télé Confiance and Rolfy Martínez say Salvadoran soldiers were seen at the stadium from 5 a.m. on 12 Nov, with troop movements near Village-de-Dieu and the Salomon market. Radio Télé Patriarche says clashes were reported in the area that morning; Afrizen says drone explosions were heard in Martissant (not yet reported by others). On the Task Force, EFE calls it an armed group serving the government "led by the American businessman Erik Prince"; AFP (France 24) calls it "a private security group serving the government." · Police, second post with photos · Le Nouvelliste, Salvadoran soldiers take position at the stadium · Gazette Haiti · Vant Bèf Info, police communiqué · EFE via Infobae, custody handed to Salvadoran troops · France 24 (AFP) · Haiti Defense Observer, video it dates to 10 Nov · Vant Bèf Info, soldiers seen from early morning · Radio Télé Patriarche, clashes reported · Afrizen, drone explosions in Martissant

Nayib Bukele (@nayibbukele), president of El Salvador; Jake Johnston (@JakobJohnston), Center for Economic and Policy Research · X, Haitian Creole, 13 Nov at 2:14 a.m. Israel time (evening of 12 Nov in Haiti); Johnston on X, English, 13 Nov at 2:56 a.m. Israel time

In Creole: "Under the bilateral agreement between the government of Haiti and the government of El Salvador, a joint operation bringing together the Haitian National Police, the Armed Forces of Haiti (FAd'H), the Task Force and the Salvadoran Special Forces succeeded in taking control of the Sylvio Cator stadium and securing it. During the operation, we discovered several human skulls inside the stadium. Now we will set up an operational base in the stadium so we can keep advancing deeper into the territory the gangs control. Now we are the ones with the advantage on the ground. I firmly ask all gang members to leave the area before the offensive begins. Those who choose to stay and fight our forces will have to suffer the consequences." He gives no detail on the skulls. Johnston: "In his latest post, Bukele shows off robots strapped with rifles being used in Haiti. The country's role as a security lab for foreign nations continues. What could go wrong …"

Evidence: Signed post with a 77-second video (not watched by us; about 576,000 views). Johnston posts a still image; Corvington Matin and Arturo McFields also say the video shows armed robots, drones and armored trucks. No photo of the skulls in the post text and no toll. · open the original

Haiti Defense Observer thanked him for crediting the Haitian army. Radio Métronome says it reported from the morning that the stadium would be a Salvadoran base. · Jake Johnston, robots · EFE via Infobae, base and ultimatum · Infobae, "several human skulls" · Corvington Matin, robots · Arturo McFields, robots and drones · Radio Métronome · Haiti Defense Observer reply

2026-11-13 04:20
Innova News (@InnovaNews7) and Infos Partage (@infospartagesos), Haitian news accounts; Haiti Defense Observer (@HT_DefenseObsvr), with photos by Patrice Noël for AP; Radio Télé Confiance (@RTConfiance) · X, French; Sylvio Cator stadium, Portail Léogâne and Village de Dieu, downtown Port-au-Prince; 12 Nov at 7:17 p.m. (Innova), 13 Nov at 12:07 a.m. (Infos Partage), 1:42 a.m. (Haiti Defense Observer) and 2:39 a.m. (Radio Télé Confiance) Israel time

Innova News says the base at the stadium will house Salvadoran special forces deployed on the front and agents of some specialized police units, and that "reconnaissance operations are under way" ahead of moves into Village-de-Dieu and Martissant, "before heading for the commune of Carrefour," held by Viv Ansanm gangs. Infos Partage reports the same, with video. Haiti Defense Observer posts AP photos of Haitian and Salvadoran soldiers at Portail Léogâne and says the position puts them right next to Village de Dieu, held by the "5 Segonn" group of Izo (Johnson André). Radio Télé Confiance says Viv Ansanm fighters are moving away from their centers of operation and that the base raises hope of reopening the road south, "from Grand-Ravine to Martissant, by way of Ti-Bois," and of hurting the boat owners it says make tens of millions of dollars a month.

Evidence: Innova News and Infos Partage name no source; Infos Partage posts a 130-second video; Haiti Defense Observer posts 4 AP photos; Radio Télé Confiance posts 4 photos and names no source for the boat figures. · open the original

Port-au-Prince Post: "The destruction of the residence of Wilson Joseph, alias Lanmò San Jou, has not changed the balance of power in the Cul-de-Sac plain." It says armed men remain visible at Carrefour Marasa, near the Famosa company, and that a gang leader known as "Chamo," described as Lanmò San Jou's right-hand man, has taken over a house near the company and lives there with his men. The Salvadoran account says Lanmò San Jou said on a live broadcast that he is not fleeing out of fear of the Salvadoran soldiers, that he left his house to keep his mother, "his greatest treasure," from being hurt, and that he would face the soldiers without fear.

Communiqué headline: "National security: the prime minister reaffirms the state's determination to retake control of the territory." Prime Minister Alix Didier Fils-Aimé visited the Téléco forward base in Kenscoff with Justice Minister Patrick Pélissier and representatives of the European Union and the Organization of American States, and restated the fight against the gangs "with the support of the Gang Suppression Force" (Le Nouvelliste). Gazette Haiti names EU ambassador Hélène Roos, OAS resident representative Catherine Pognat and Kenscoff deputy mayor Jean Max Duvivier. The police posted a photo captioned "Visit of the PNH's first FOB" (forward operating base).

2026-11-13 03:30
Pierre Espérance, executive director of the National Human Rights Defense Network (RNDDH), as reported by Magazine Haïti; AlterPresse (@AlterPresse); sociologist Michèle Oriol interviewed by Nancy Roc · Magazine Haïti on X, 12 Nov at 9:13 p.m. Israel time, on remarks of 11 Nov; AlterPresse on X, 13 Nov at 12:14 a.m. Israel time; Nancy Roc's Substack, relayed by Juno7, 10 Nov

Espérance denied reports that Washington had asked El Salvador to slow its troops' operations in Haiti; he says the United States is asking for better coordination of the forces and calls the reports "a disinformation campaign aimed at protecting the gangs." AlterPresse headlines: "Several organizations denounce American pressure against the Salvadoran offensive targeting the gangs." Oriol, asked "Who wants to rein in Bukele in Haiti, and why?", blames decades of international involvement: "The very notion of authority and hierarchy has disappeared," and Haiti, "without a national defense system, was crushed by the explosion of drug trafficking."

Evidence: Espérance: named person, as relayed with a photo. AlterPresse: headline only; its site blocked us (bot check, then "access denied"), so we could not read which organizations or what they said. Oriol: named person, on the record. · open the original

2026-11-13 03:20
ALBA Movimientos and the International Assembly of the Peoples (IPA), left-wing networks, joint statement; carried by PouSiOuPaTKonnen · IPA website, English; PouSiOuPaTKonnen, French, received 11 Nov

"BUKELE OUT OF HAITI!" The groups call the deployment "a violation of the Haitian people's right to self-determination" and say "Bukele, in coordination with Marco Rubio, launched an illegal occupation operation." "There is no Security Council resolution authorizing this deployment, no endorsement from the OAS, no withdrawal date, and no known cost. Washington did not send troops; it sent a C-130 aircraft from the Southern Command, Trump's public endorsement, and Marco Rubio himself." They compare it to MINUSTAH and the Kenyan-led mission.

2026-11-13 03:10
Global Centre for the Responsibility to Protect (GCR2P), Atrocity Alert No. 502; relayed by Magazine Haïti; ACLED analyst María Fernanda Arocha · GCR2P website, English, 11 Nov at 8:19 p.m. Israel time; ACLED website (comment undated on the page; it covers events through 8 Nov)

GCR2P says the use of "heavy weapons, attack helicopters and drones in densely populated areas of Port-au-Prince has significantly heightened risks to civilians," lists Haitian forces, the Gang Suppression Force, "a private military contractor" and self-defense groups among the actors, and says "the UN has indicated that most of the additional Salvadoran troops are operating outside the GSF's mandate and monitoring mechanisms." It cites Médecins Sans Frontières treating women and children wounded by gunfire and drone explosions, and asks for clear command arrangements and independent investigation of drone harm. ACLED's Arocha says clashes and drone strikes against gangs rose 45.8% in the 21 days after the Salvadoran deployment began compared with the 21 days before, mostly by the police and the Task Force, and that estimated deaths in these operations "already surpass 100, of which at least 26 have been identified as civilians."

Evidence: Named organizations; GCR2P cites RNDDH, MSF, BINUH and the UN Panel of Experts; ACLED cites its own event data. · open the original
Map: Port-au-Prince (18.547°N, 72.340°W) (city center)

Forbin met Kogri on 11 Nov to strengthen security cooperation; the two said they want a more structured bilateral dialogue. Chad has soldiers in the Gang Suppression Force. Magazine Haïti says Forbin told the forum the international community must finally act with determination in Haiti.

2026-11-13 02:40
National Commission for Disarmament, Dismantlement and Reintegration (CNDDR), as reported by Le Nouvelliste and AlterPresse; former senator Gracia Delva, letter reported by Vant Bèf Info · Port-au-Prince, 12 Nov; Marchand-Dessalines (Artibonite), letter dated 10 Nov

The CNDDR says it has set up departmental networks across the country to work with local authorities and community groups on disarmament, reintegration and violence prevention. Separately, Gracia Delva, head of the Ayiti Anvan party, wrote to the prime minister asking for more police and targeted operations against the armed groups in Marchand-Dessalines, citing attacks, threats, forced displacement and blocked roads. Vant Bèf Info notes Delva has been under Canadian sanctions since June 2023, which he rejects, and that the prime minister's office has not answered.

Evidence: Commission statement; named former senator's letter as reported. · open the original

2026-11-12 04:50
RFI (Grégory Genevrier), French; Jake Johnston (@JakobJohnston), Center for Economic and Policy Research; Bilawal Sidhu (@bilawalsidhu), US tech commentator; replies to Bukele by Haitian X users · RFI web, 11 Nov at 5:59 p.m. Israel time; Johnston on X, 11 Nov at 8:50 p.m. and 12 Nov at 1:57 a.m. and 3:52 a.m. Israel time; the strike site geolocated by RFI to Thomazeau commune, east of Port-au-Prince

RFI geolocated Bukele's 48-second video and says the helicopters, light MD 500E types (at least three on screen), took off from Port-au-Prince airport and hit several buildings 17 km to the east, in Thomazeau commune. It traces the targeting to X: on 5 Nov a user posted a photo of a luxury house said to belong to a gang leader ("Nayib Bukele, raze it for us"), later deleted; on 6 Nov Bukele asked for "the exact address"; on 7 Nov an anonymous open-source analyst who goes by "Stinky" posted GPS coordinates. "Stinky" told RFI he answered because most people posting locations were wrong, and that his work "was used to target people who, to put it mildly, are not good people. So I have no regrets." RFI says it is not known how many were killed or wounded, or who was nearby, and that the video has more than 11 million views. Haitian users keep replying to Bukele with what they say are the houses of other gang leaders, among them Jeff "Gwo Lwa" in Canaan.

Evidence: RFI's own geolocation of the video, with screenshots of the deleted post and Bukele's reply; one anonymous analyst interviewed by handle. No casualty figures. · open the original
Another source says: Another source says (Jake Johnston): "The location of the house was no big secret. It was built in recent years right out in the open... Half of these guy's houses are labeled on Google Maps." Replying to Sidhu, who called the X-to-airstrike chain "the everything app," Johnston wrote that the house "was hit previously by local forces working with Erik Prince," pointing to the Miami Herald. Another source says on the date: Le Nouvelliste, citing a government source, says the house was heavily damaged on Tuesday, 10 Nov; HaitiLibre says strikes were still going on Tuesday morning; the Miami Herald dated the strike to early 8 Nov. · Jake Johnston, "no big secret" · Jake Johnston reply to Bilawal Sidhu · Bilawal Sidhu, "the everything app" · Le Nouvelliste, Salvadoran helicopters, 10 Nov · HaitiLibre FLASH, rockets on the house · RFI on X

2026-11-12 04:40
The New Humanitarian and AyiboPost (Daniela Mohor, Jerôme Wendy Norestyl, Wethzer Piercin), quoting Lucien Jura (Haitian government secretary of state for communication), an unnamed source close to the UN, an unnamed source close to the force, ACLED (Sandra Pellegrini), MSF (Davina Hayles), Monique Clesca, Jake Johnston and others; shared by Johnston on X · Port-au-Prince and Santiago, Chile; web, English, 11 Nov at 3:35 p.m. Israel time

Asked about the agreement behind the Salvadoran deployment, Lucien Jura "denied there was any agreement," while saying the Salvadoran troops, like all contributing countries, "operate under the auspices of the GSF in close coordination with the Haitian government." A source close to the force says only about 200 to 300 Salvadorans are part of it, the rest outside its command, and that talks on logistics and "deconfliction" continue. The article says the Salvadorans have one main base on a property El Salvador rented in Tabarre and a much smaller one next to the U.S. embassy residence compound. ACLED counts at least 21 people killed in security operations in Croix-des-Bouquets between 18 Oct and 2 Nov; MSF says it received about 30 patients with bullet and blast wounds in the eight days after 18 Oct, about what it would normally see in a month. A 20-year-old in Carrefour Fouji (Croix-des-Bouquets) says gangs stop people leaving with suitcases, force passers-by to build barricades and recruit young people by force. Jake Johnston says he counted at least 30 charter flights from El Salvador, the last on 3 Nov, and finds it "hard to believe" El Salvador pays for it alone. "Bukele has spoken to Haitians more often in the last two weeks than our leaders in the past two years," Monique Clesca says, adding: "this is not his backyard." A UN-linked source: "It's not the typical human rights setting. It's combat."

Evidence: Named officials and analysts; two anonymous sources; one resident under a changed name; ACLED and MSF figures; satellite imagery of the Tabarre base. · open the original
Another source says: Another source says: Prime Minister Fils-Aimé said on 10 Nov that the two governments signed a partnership agreement about three weeks ago (Enpak News, Le Nouvelliste); Vice President Ulloa says El Salvador has "all the necessary agreements." Rubio put the Salvadorans in the force at about 100 (Listín Diario); HaitiLibre, citing AP, says 125 of at least 2,500. · Jake Johnston sharing the article · Listín Diario, Rubio on the two missions
Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W) · Carrefour (18.535°N, 72.409°W) · Tabarre (18.562°N, 72.251°W)
2026-11-12 04:30
BINUH (@BINUH_UN), the UN Integrated Office in Haiti, quarterly human-rights report; head of office Carlos Ruiz Massieu; relayed by Le Nouvelliste and Magazine Haïti (@maghaiti) · Port-au-Prince; report (PDF, English and French) and X, 11 Nov at 4:26 p.m. Israel time; covers 1 July to 30 September 2026

The UN's figures: at least 1,167 people killed and 459 injured from July to September; 45 percent by gangs, 5 percent by self-defense groups and "Bwa Kale" mobs, and 50 percent during security force operations against gangs. It describes ground operations by police units, sometimes with the army and "a government-established 'task force' composed of police officers and personnel from a foreign private military company," and explosive drone strikes by the same task force, concentrated downtown, in Carrefour-Feuilles, Fort Saint-Clair and La Saline against Grand Ravine and Village de Dieu, and in Gressier, Kenscoff, Pétion-Ville and the Lower Artibonite against Kokorat San Ras and Gran Grif. Fourteen residents with no gang ties, two of them children, were killed or injured by drones. In Gressier, at least 165 gang members, 24 of them boys aged 14 to 16, were killed in exchanges of fire with police from 12 Aug to 30 Sep. Police were involved in 44 extrajudicial executions or attempts (14 the quarter before). Gangs returned to Bel-Air, Bas Delmas and La Saline; kidnappings in the capital rose to 92 victims. BINUH says it has "received no information" that any investigation was opened into deaths in security operations, "including those involving personnel from a private military company." Ruiz Massieu: the "scale of massacres" shows the need for State presence "in recently liberated areas."

Evidence: UN document with counts by place and month; no photos or named victims in the summary. The period ends before the Salvadoran deployment. · open the original

A $24 million deal for Fencebridge Strategic Risk, led by Bryan Seaver, to supply the Haitian police with semi-automatic rifles and ammunition has been only partly delivered, and at least $7 million of Haitian public money is missing, last traced to an escrow account tied to a Texas woman, Ellie Soto, whom Texas courts found had defrauded investors and who was indicted for securities fraud in 2022. Haitian sources say they sent at least $15 million; Fencebridge says it received about half. Fencebridge blames the escrow attorney and Soto. The Herald says the shortfall delayed supplies "as armed gangs launched deadly attacks and police were running out of bullets."

Axios (Marc Caputo), citing diplomatic notes, a copy of a text message and unnamed sources; Jake Johnston (@JakobJohnston), Center for Economic and Policy Research; Diario Libre; Centre à la Une (@Centrealaune); Magazine Haïti (@maghaiti) · Axios web, Washington, 10 Nov; Johnston on X at 2:43 p.m. and 8:33 p.m. Israel time; Dominican and Haitian web and X, Spanish and Creole

Axios lays out a written quarrel between the State Department and El Salvador. On 4 Nov, U.S. chargé d'affaires David Howell texted Prime Minister Fils-Aimé that the Salvadorans were not "coordinating" with the Gang Suppression Force: "They took at least three casualties. They don't have a medical plan. They nearly killed GSF troops as I understand it," and asked him to have El Salvador stop operations "until they coordinate." On 5 Nov the U.S. embassy in San Salvador sent a formal protest note saying "Salvadoran small arms fire" struck the U.S. embassy in Port-au-Prince during the 31 Oct convoy attack, "nearly hitting U.S. personnel." On 9 Nov El Salvador answered with four points: its troops were firing away from the embassy; it did not fire toward the Chadian soldiers of the force on 3 Nov; it has coordinated "air assets" with the force and Haitian officials since 26 Oct; and it "categorically denies" the three casualties, saying the false report "generates serious doubts about the credibility" of the rest. A U.S. official told Axios that "a class of bureaucrats" opposes the change President Trump wants. Jake Johnston says the deployment is "exposing the divisions in Washington": Trump gave Bukele the go-ahead, while Rubio and the State Department are angry. He notes Rubio said on 9 Nov there is "no plan B" if the Gang Suppression Force fails, without mentioning El Salvador, "a real clear 'shots fired' moment."

Evidence: Documents shown to Axios (the text message and both diplomatic notes) and unnamed U.S. and Salvadoran sources; Axios did not publish the documents. Johnston gives his reading of public remarks and the Axios report. · open the original

"There is no trace of them to determine whether they are alive or not," Ulloa said of the four Salvadoran airmen missing since the 18 Oct ambush. He said Bukele, as commander in chief, follows the operation "minute by minute"; that the troops freed a church sheltering mostly women and girls and supported Chadian soldiers under attack; that the mission is a bilateral agreement with Haiti, coordinated with the Haitian police, not under UN command; that El Salvador pays for it alone; and that "the objective is to rescue our compatriots, and once we do, we go back."

Evidence: Named official on video (YouTube interview), as reported by two Salvadoran outlets. No new information on where the four are. · open the original

Miami Herald (Jacqueline Charles), citing several unnamed sources; relayed in Haiti by Le Facteur Haïti, Le Placentin, L'Observatoire, La Tribune News and Satellite509, and in Creole by VORIXA Magazine citing Carel Pèdre (@carelpedre); Rolfy Martínez (@RolfyMartinez), relaying Igor Rugwiza, spokesman of the Gang Suppression Force, and commentator Rudy Sanon · Croix-des-Bouquets (Plaine du Cul-de-Sac); Miami Herald web, 9 Nov at 11:13 a.m. Eastern (6:13 p.m. Israel time); Haitian web and X, French and Creole; Dominican X, Spanish

The Miami Herald gives the fullest account yet of the 8 Nov strike on the house used by Wilson Joseph ("Lanmò San Jou"), leader of 400 Mawozo. It says the police drone Task Force, "which uses explosive drones operated by Haitian police officers with the support of private security contractors," hit the house at about 2:30 a.m. Sunday (Haiti time) while Task Force members moved on the ground. Four helicopters flew overhead: two flown by the Task Force and two by Salvadoran troops, one source said. Joseph was not there; the house had been under surveillance, and his gunmen inside, "possibly as many as 20," were killed, two sources said. The Herald says the house, with indoor and outdoor pools, appears to have been built in the last year, and that this is the second time the Task Force, which works out of the prime minister's office with Vectus Global, has destroyed a gang leader's home (after Jimmy "Barbecue" Chérizier's houses in January). It also says several sources blame the 18 Oct ambush of the four Salvadorans on Chen Mechan, reinforced by 400 Mawozo. Le Facteur Haïti, which says it broke the story, now headlines "at least 20 mawozo" killed and says he had already fled to Carrefour. Rolfy Martínez posted four photos of a collapsed building and a 7-second clip he calls "supposed images" of the house.

Evidence: Several unnamed sources (Miami Herald): one for the helicopters, two for the deaths. No official statement and no official toll from the Haitian police, the Task Force or El Salvador (Le Placentin and L'Observatoire say the same). El País reports that the roughly 2,000 Salvadoran soldiers sent by Bukele act alongside the Gang Suppression Force "under orders of a three-star general who answers directly to Nayib Bukele," with no public agreement on rules, immunity or limits. 2026-11-10 04:20
Amnesty International (Ana Piquer, Americas director) · Amnesty web, Spanish, 9 Nov

Amnesty says Prime Minister Fils-Aimé and President Bukele must make sure all Salvadoran operations in Haiti respect international human rights law. It asks both governments to publish the agreement behind the deployment and explain how the troops are supervised, what rules govern the use of force, to whom they answer, and how residents can report abuses without fear. It warns about operations near homes, schools and hospitals, asks for trained staff and protocols for children recruited by gangs, and calls for the four Salvadorans missing since 18 Oct to be freed unharmed if a gang holds them. "The response to gang violence cannot reproduce the abuses and impunity that marked earlier interventions in Haiti," Piquer says.

Evidence: Named official statement; no new field evidence. · open the original

National Human Rights Defense Network (RNDDH), report carried by the Miami Herald (Jacqueline Charles), Fact Checking News (FCN) and Reuters; Haitian National Police (@pnh_officiel); Al Jazeera · Cité Soleil (Nan Nélio, Pierre-Six, Damien, Sarthe); Tabarre, Croix-des-Missions, Santo and the Cul-de-Sac plain

The police posted in Creole early on 7 Nov (Israel time) that "the PNH and other forces neutralized a big terrorist gang chief," with a photo. Al Jazeera, citing the police statement, says Mathias Saintil was hit by "multiple bullets" and died later in hospital. The rights group RNDDH gives a different account: on the night of 5 Nov, between 9 and 10 p.m., a kamikaze drone hit his home and the vehicle he was sitting in at Nan Nélio as he left for a street party in Pierre-Six; he was hurt in the abdomen, moved from a hospital in Belécourt to one in Canaan, and died there about 5:30 p.m. on 6 Nov (FCN adds: in an area held by Jeff Gwo Lwa). RNDDH says at least seven kamikaze drones were used that night across Tabarre, Croix-des-Missions, Santo, Cité Soleil and the Cul-de-Sac plain. In Pierre-Six, it says, a drone hit a gang party that residents also attended; three civilians died on the spot and ten were wounded, two of whom died overnight. It says about fifteen gang members were killed in Damien and Sarthe, and that a pickup carrying fifteen armed men of Ti Gabriel (Brooklyn gang) on their way to reinforce Mathias was stopped by the task force: five killed, six wounded. The Miami Herald published a photo of his burning vehicle.

Evidence: Named human rights group report, seen by Reuters and the Miami Herald; police post with photo; handout photo of the vehicle (Miami Herald). The casualty details come from RNDDH alone. · open the original
Another source says: Another source says (police statement, via Al Jazeera and FCN) he died of gunshot wounds, not a drone. Another source says (Latinar, Russian think tank Rybar's Latin America unit) the Boston gang was a neighborhood group of about 100 men and the government is overstating the blow. Counts of dead differ between FCN's and the Miami Herald's summaries of the same RNDDH report. · Haitian police post (Creole) · FCN, RNDDH details · Reuters · Al Jazeera · Le Nouvelliste · Latinar · Le Facteur Haïti, vehicle photo

Ulloa told EFE on 6 Nov that Prime Minister Fils-Aimé will travel to El Salvador on 16 and 17 Nov for a conference on the state of democracy, and "there we will have the chance to discuss what the future of this force is"; no return date for the troops is set. The Guardian (Tom Phillips), quoting Vanda Felbab-Brown (Brookings), Adam Isacson (WOLA), Romain Le Cour (Global Initiative) and Bill O'Neill (UN expert on human rights in Haiti); Pirate Wire Services (Joshua Collins) · Web; Port-au-Prince and Washington

Felbab-Brown: "there's a huge craving for a strongman who [can] deliver Haiti from the gangs," and the fighting could turn into "really, really, really tough urban fighting." Isacson sees the operation partly as a way for Bukele to build his crime-fighting image before El Salvador's February presidential election. Le Cour calls the five forces "an orchestra without a conductor" and says "with drones, attack helicopters and heavy weapons, the confrontation has changed dramatically in a matter of days." O'Neill: "It's all very opaque and unpredictable... it potentially [could get] very ugly and very dangerous." The Guardian says the Salvadorans are setting up a base near the U.S. Embassy. Pirate Wire Services asks who answers for abuses with five forces acting on their own.

Evidence: Named analysts on the record; no new field reporting on events. · open the original

Le Concret Info says seven kamikaze drones were used and that dozens of armed men, including some tied to Ti Gabriel, were also killed (unnamed sources). Associated Press (via WTOP); 400 Mawozo statement to AP; Haiti's National Commission for Disarmament, Demobilization and Reintegration; Nayib Bukele (@nayibbukele) · San Juan (AP filing); Croix-des-Bouquets / Lanmò San Jou's house (address sought on X)

On 6 Nov, 400 Mawozo sent AP a three-page note saying it had reached out to Haiti's National Commission for Disarmament, Demobilization and Reintegration for "peace, stability and national reconciliation," that "dialogue is not a capitulation," and that it plans a peaceful demonstration later this month to open national talks. An anonymous gang spokesperson told AP the group will negotiate and that disarmament is a process; what it wants in return is peace and an end to what it calls "international interference," and a pause in military operations in some areas. The commission told AP someone contacted it on the gang's behalf but that any dialogue must follow the government's roadmap. The same note complains about social-media posts targeting Lanmò San Jou's house, including comments attributed to Bukele. On 6 Nov Bukele answered a tagged post in Creole: "Kote kay sa a ye? 2026-11-07 02:20
Infobae (Henry Heredia), citing Romain Le Cour Grandmaison (Global Initiative Against Transnational Organized Crime / Haiti Observatory) · Web; San Salvador filing on Haiti

A 6 Nov analysis says the Salvadoran contingent faces urban war on ground it does not know, without Creole and without its own intelligence networks, against groups that know every street and alliance. Le Cour Grandmaison tells BBC Mundo (as quoted) that Bukele's El Salvador model was mass imprisonment, not foreign military operations, and that Haiti now has five armed structures — police, army, Gang Suppression Force, a special force with Vectus Global private contractors, and the Salvadorans — each with its own mandate and no shared plan. He puts the main gangs at about 15,000 to 20,000 members and says Viv Ansanm has been fragmenting for six or seven months; pressure could deepen that split or pull them back together.

Evidence: Named analyst quoted; Infobae and BBC Mundo packaging · open the original

2026-11-03 02:53
AP, reporter Dánica Coto (@danicacoto), interview with Wilson Joseph ("Lanmò San Jou"), leader of 400 Mawozo; summary by NAGO.TV · AP (Washington Post copy) and NAGO.TV, Haiti

The 400 Mawozo leader told AP on Monday that he did write to UN Secretary-General Guterres asking for a delegation to come to Haiti for dialogue and mediation; AP calls it his first interview with a major international news company. According to NAGO.TV's summary of the AP story, he says he is ready to lay down his weapons and sets terms for a peace commission, says Haitian politicians armed the gangs, denies killing innocent people and says drone strikes kill civilians. He told AP the four missing Salvadorans are dead and that the attack happened in Chen Mechan territory; he gave no evidence. The Haitian government says the four were abducted, and the Salvadoran government believes they are alive. AP says fighting has intensified in the Cul-de-Sac plain between armed men and the Gang Suppression Force, police, soldiers and Vectus Global. He has been under UN sanctions since 8 Dec 2023 and is under US indictment over the 2021 kidnapping of missionaries. Rezo Nodwes says the AP story backs what it reported on Sunday night.

Evidence: Named gang leader, interviewed by a named reporter (phone or in person not stated); no evidence for his claim about the four · open the original
Another source says: Another source says: a search summary of the AP story quotes UN spokesman Stéphane Dujarric as saying the UN received a letter bearing his signature, and that receipt does not mean the UN endorses it or recognizes him; NAGO.TV says the UN had not responded publicly. The AP site itself was blocked to us. · NAGO.TV summary · Dánica Coto post · Rezo Nodwes post

2026-11-02 04:20
Diario Libre (@DiarioLibre), Dominican daily, citing the Dominican foreign ministry · Diario Libre, Port of Haina, Dominican Republic

A ship left the Port of Haina on Sunday 1 Nov for Haiti carrying about 525 tonnes of supplies, weapons, specialized vehicles and support equipment for the Gang Suppression Force. The cargo came from Bangladesh, and Germany paid to move it to the Dominican Republic. Daniela Kroslak, UN Assistant Secretary-General and head of the UN Support Office in Haiti (UNSOH), called Dominican logistical help "essential." Dominican vice ministers Rubén Silié and Ramón Pérez Fermín and German Ambassador Maike Friedrichsen attended. Listín Diario and El Diario de Hoy, citing the Dominican foreign ministry, quote Kroslak: the shipment "includes containers, vehicles and any other military equipment." El Diario de Hoy notes that the ministry did not say how many vehicles there are or which units will get them. Le Quotidien 509 adds that command structure and coordination for the force are still being worked out, and that Kroslak raised the need for housing, food and medical care for the troops.

The attackers came mainly from Cabaret, with reinforcements from Kenscoff and the Cul-de-Sac plain; police were fighting them at Thoman near the town center, and Carriès is also threatened. The groups are said to be tied to Izo 5 Segond (part of Cabaret) and Jeff Gwo Lwa (Montrouis).

2026-10-30 01:24
Jacqueline Charles, Miami Herald · Miami Herald, Port-au-Prince and the lower Artibonite

The Salvadoran contingent has grown from a 125-member aviation unit to at least 2,000 troops (special forces, marines, infantry and support) and was expected to reach about 2,500 by the end of Thursday, against 2,526 for the whole Gang Suppression Force before the surge. Most of the Salvadorans have not had the force's two-week human rights course, and sources say no status-of-forces agreement has been signed. The Herald counts five security structures now at work: the Haitian police, the Haitian army, the Gang Suppression Force, a drone task force using contractors linked to Erik Prince, and the Salvadorans. The troops stay at a camp near the US Embassy. Former defense minister Jean Dorneval: "They need to have a legal framework or else they are mercenaries." Vanda Felbab-Brown of Brookings warns about the rules of engagement and the civilian toll of drone strikes. In the lower Artibonite, Bertide Horace welcomes Bukele while Gran Grif has pressed an offensive on Petite Rivière since 19 Oct, with bodies in the river and armed men in the town center. Jean Gilles Bernard Camille says a Salvadoran helicopter was nearly shot down. Doctors Without Borders has suspended its mobile clinics.

Carel Pèdre (@carelpedre), Haitian radio and TV host · X, in Creole

A timeline of how slow the last foreign help was: Ariel Henry asked for help in October 2022, the UN Security Council authorized the Kenya-led mission in October 2023, and the first Kenyan police arrived on 25 June 2024, nearly 20 months later. Bukele, by contrast, mobilized in a few days. Pèdre grants the two operations have different legal and logistical frames, but says the gap shows the problem "was never only a lack of means" but of political will and leadership. About 8,900 views.

2026-10-27 00:08
Presidency of the Dominican Republic, on President Luis Abinader's weekly press program (LA Semanal, Monday 26 Oct) · Official website, Spanish

Abinader: "We will act in coordination with the government of Haiti, but we will not allow those gangs within 20 kilometers of the border." He says the border is at its highest level of protection and the Dominican Republic is not at its moment of greatest risk. Defense Minister Lt. Gen. Carlos Antonio Fernández Onofre says the gangs control at least 70 percent of Port-au-Prince, people are moving mainly west rather than toward the border, at least two Haitian self-defense groups stand between the nearest gangs and the border, and intelligence shows no organized gangs in the north near Ouanaminthe (Juana Méndez). Foreign Minister Víctor "Ito" Bisonó says the Dominican Republic does not take part directly in operations, as a neighbor, but gives the GSF logistics: transit for contingents, medical evacuations, air permits, blood shipments and moving military cargo.
