El Salvador's operation

  • 13 Nov: Salvadoran troops took over the Sylvio Cator stadium downtown on 12 Nov after a police and Task Force operation and are setting up a base there (police; Le Nouvelliste counts more than twenty armored vehicles and trucks inside). Bukele says Salvadoran special forces took part, "several human skulls" were found, and gangs must leave "before the offensive begins"; Jake Johnston says the video shows robots armed with rifles. Soldiers were seen from 5 a.m. near Village-de-Dieu and the Salomon market (Vant Bèf Info, Radio Télé Confiance). Innova News says reconnaissance is under way toward Village-de-Dieu and Martissant, then Carrefour. Haitians were filmed greeting the soldiers and kissing the Salvadoran flag; Magazine Haïti says marches in their support are called for 18 Nov. ALBA Movimientos calls the deployment an "illegal occupation." The four airmen are still missing.
    Map: Village de Dieu (18.538°N, 72.350°W) (neighborhood, approximate point) · Martissant (18.527°N, 72.357°W) (neighborhood, approximate point) · Carrefour (18.535°N, 72.409°W)
  • 12 Nov: RFI geolocates Bukele's strike video to Thomazeau and says the targeting rested on a photo and GPS coordinates posted on X by anonymous users; Jake Johnston says the house was "no big secret" and had been hit before. Vant Bèf Info says the troops resumed operations in the Cul-de-Sac plain on 10 Nov after a pause over coordination and intelligence sharing (no named source); Radio Télé Confiance posts video of their helicopters over the plain. The New Humanitarian and AyiboPost: a source close to the force says only 200 to 300 Salvadorans are part of it; government spokesman Lucien Jura says there is no agreement with El Salvador (the prime minister spoke of one on 10 Nov); the main base is a rented property in Tabarre, with a smaller one next to the U.S. embassy residence compound. Bukele answered Amnesty USA with "Ratioed." Laurent Lamothe proposes the Salvadorans take Port-au-Prince and Croix-des-Bouquets. The four airmen are still missing.
    Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W) · Port-au-Prince (18.547°N, 72.340°W) (city center) · Thomazeau (18.652°N, 72.094°W) · Tabarre (18.562°N, 72.251°W)
  • 11 Nov: Bukele posted a 48-second video in Creole on 10 Nov: "We kept our promise… We destroyed the house of Lanmò San Jou and the headquarters of the 400 Mawozo gang," saying most gang members ran off without a fight; he gave no toll and did not say who else took part. Le Repère says Salvadoran soldiers with drones did it on 10 Nov and killed nearly 20; the Miami Herald puts it on 8 Nov and credits the police Task Force. Axios says El Salvador's 9 Nov note to Washington denies hitting the U.S. embassy or the Chadian soldiers, says it has coordinated "air assets" with the Gang Suppression Force since 26 Oct, "categorically denies" three Salvadoran casualties and questions the U.S. chargé d'affaires' "credibility." Vice President Ulloa: "no trace" of the four missing airmen; their armored vehicle has been recovered; the mission is bilateral, paid for by El Salvador, and ends when they are found. Dominican President Abinader says his country gives the Salvadorans intelligence and logistics.
  • 10 Nov: the Miami Herald says two of the four helicopters over the 8 Nov strike on Lanmò San Jou's house were flown by Salvadoran troops (one unnamed source). El País says the roughly 2,000 Salvadorans answer to a three-star general who reports to Bukele, with no published rules, and that two have been wounded by gunfire. Amnesty asks both governments to publish the deployment agreement. KILBA asks the UN to send the Salvadorans to the Lower Artibonite first; a Mirebalais native asks Bukele to free his town. Thomassin marched for Bukele on 9 Nov. Cartel Watch posts foot-patrol video in the Cul-de-Sac plain and repeats the "Kenken" capture story, which no Haitian outlet has reported. The four missing Salvadorans are still missing.
    Map: Artibonite (19.337°N, 72.492°W) (department, approximate center) · Mirebalais (18.834°N, 72.105°W)
  • 9 Nov: Haitian outlets, citing police sources, say drone strikes with helicopters overhead destroyed the Croix-des-Bouquets house of Lanmò San Jou at about 3 a.m. on 8 Nov (Haiti time); Rolfy Martínez calls it a Salvadoran operation, Vant Bèf Info lists the police, the Gang Suppression Force, the Task Force and Salvadoran forces. No official word from El Salvador. PrécisNouvèl says three boats of fleeing gang members sank after Salvadoran helicopters intervened on 7 Nov (not yet reported by others). El Diario de Hoy: day 21, the four missing Salvadorans are still missing. AlterPresse headline: Ulloa says some gang members are considering surrender.
    Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W)
  • 8 Nov: Vice President Ulloa tells EFE the troops "have been cleaning the zone" in Croix-des-Bouquets, that El Salvador pays the whole cost (Bukele earlier said 90 percent) and that Prime Minister Fils-Aimé will visit El Salvador on 16 and 17 Nov to discuss "the future of this force"; no return date is set. Drone video of a white flag on the compound tagged as Lanmò San Jou's spread on 7 Nov after Bukele asked for its address; the gang's own letter dates the flag to 20 Aug. Bukele reposted rally videos from Canapé-Vert and students thanking him. A U.S. C-5 and a C-130 landed at Comalapa on 5 Nov with vehicles; nobody official has said whether the cargo is for Haiti (El Testigo). Cartel Watch relays a rumor from "El Jos" that the four were killed, which it calls unverified itself; the four are still missing. Alexander Gálvez claims more than 100 gang members killed (Sivar News; no source).
    Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W)
  • 7 Nov: Bukele, in Creole on 6 Nov, asks X users for the exact address of a house tagged as Lanmò San Jou's: "Ban nou adrès egzak la, n ap detwi l" ("Give us the exact address, and we will destroy it"). Jake Johnston says at least two Salvadoran troops in Haiti have been seriously wounded (multiple unnamed sources; Radio Télé Confiance relays). Viral "Kenken" capture claims with alleged Salvadoran help were called fake by Radio Télé Confiance and walked back elsewhere. Magazine Haïti, Rolfy Martínez and Cartel Watch show crowds chanting Bukele; France 24 quotes residents who back the troops but fear speaking out. Infobae / Le Cour Grandmaison: five forces, no shared plan. The four remain missing.
  • 6 Nov: Ulloa tells the Ibero-American Summit in Madrid the Salvadoran troops are "not an occupation force" but a bilateral arrangement, aims to recover the four alive and help crush the gangs, and claims rescues of church hostages and aid to Chadian troops (Infobae, EFE, Centre à la Une). Forbin met Ulloa the same day (Haitian MFA via Infobae; Diario El Salvador covers Hill Tinoco–Forbin). Haitian army trucks and Salvadoran armor left the airport at Clercine toward Croix-des-Bouquets or Tabarre (Haiti Defense Observer video, 5 Nov; Rolfy Martínez similar). The four remain missing.
    Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W) · Tabarre (18.562°N, 72.251°W)
  • 5 Nov: the police say a joint operation with the force and Salvadoran soldiers found the burned vehicle of the four missing Salvadorans at Santo, Croix-des-Bouquets (on Monday 2 Nov per El Diario de Hoy and Média Libre); the four are still missing and the police say nothing about what was inside. On 4 Nov Salvadoran soldiers patrolled on foot at Carrefour Rita, in Tabarre and in Clercine near the airport, with helicopters overhead (Infos Partage, Parrain Infos, Haiti Defense Observer). Bukele posted the videos with "Sanble gang yo pa vle goumen avè n" ("Looks like the gangs don't want to fight us"); his press office gave a different Spanish version. A man presented as the presumed 400 Mawozo leader says on video he will "attack with everything I have" if Bukele will not negotiate (primiciainftv; not yet reported by others). In Madrid on 3 Nov, Foreign Minister Alexandra Hill Tinoco told Haiti's Raina Forbin that support continues, "notamment au sein de la FRG" (Le Nouvelliste). Edwin Paraison urges Bukele to put the troops formally under the force (El Día). Rolfy Martínez posted video he says shows the "united forces with El Salvador" fighting at Arcahaie; the mayor credits only police and brigades.
    Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W) · Carrefour (18.535°N, 72.409°W) · Arcahaie (18.770°N, 72.512°W) · Tabarre (18.562°N, 72.251°W)
  • 4 Nov: Bukele says Salvadoran attack helicopters drove off gangs attacking Chadian troops of the Gang Suppression Force on the evening of 3 Nov in Croix-des-Bouquets and Tabarre, the first time he has described them in combat (on 1 Nov he said they had not flown a mission); night videos show a helicopter firing (Rolfy Martínez, Visegrád 24, Infos Partage, Corvington Matin). Another source says the words come from a force report (EFE via El Nuevo Diario). Bukele also wrote to Haitians in Creole: "nou pa anvayisè" ("we are not invaders"). El Salvador Times says the troops reached Chen Mechan's base in Santo and found it empty, with no clue to the four; En el Foco says 14 hostages were freed there (no source; not yet reported by others). A video shows a Santo crowd chanting "Bukele." HaitiInfoProj says journalists are barred from filming patrols. Col. José Roberto Figueroa, said to lead the Salvadoran ground troops, stood beside the prime minister on 3 Nov (Diario El Mundo). Latinar says pro-government accounts are crediting the troops with Galaxie's death, which the Haitian police carried out.
    Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W) · Tabarre (18.562°N, 72.251°W)
  • 3 Nov: the troops launched their first big operation on 2 Nov in the Cul-de-Sac plain, with about ten armored vehicles, against Chen Mechan and 400 Mawozo (EFE); Haitian outlets report about ten gang members killed at Santo and Croix-des-Missions. Bukele quote-posted a Creole video "Here are Bukele's men on the ground" and reposted EFE and Bloomberg, with no statement of his own. A soldier on video asks people not to film the troops (Rolfy Martínez). Radio YSUCA says leave has been suspended for soldiers and police ahead of a new contingent (via Planet News HT; not yet reported by others). Michael Paarlberg calls the deployment "a publicity stunt with no clear military objective."
  • 2 Nov: troops patrolled Tabarre for a third day, near Santo, where the four were ambushed, and some passers-by applauded (Diario El Mundo; Alerta Noticias video). On 1 Nov Haitian army (FAd'H) trucks escorted Salvadoran personnel to their Tabarre garrison, which Haiti Defense Observer reads as a joint effort with the Haitian army, with no defense accord known yet. Bukele posted only reposts overnight, including a patrol photo, and nothing new on the four. Jake Johnston and Dr. H. Salazar read his "Can confirm" to mean the mission is not a rescue; no Salvadoran deaths have been announced. Edwin Paraison, a former Haitian consul, says some worry the troops answer to their own command, not the Gang Suppression Force. Videos show residents handing water to soldiers (Eduardo Menoni, Caleb de Ra Paul) and a crowd asking Bukele to intervene (Rolfy Martínez). UNA-Haiti's head, Hervé Dupiton, says he is being attacked online since Bukele's denial.
    Map: Tabarre (18.562°N, 72.251°W)
  • 1 Nov: Trump said on video at Mar-a-Lago that Bukele told him "They killed some of his people" and that he answered "You have to do what you have to do"; Bukele quote-posted it with "Can confirm." No Salvadoran deaths have been reported and the fate of the four is still unknown (Drop Site, Rolfy Martínez). Bukele denied UNA-Haiti's bombing charge: the mission "doesn't even have bombs," and the attack helicopters arrived on 29 Oct and "haven't flown a single mission." Salvadoran troops made their first street patrols on Friday 30 Oct in Tabarre and at Clercine near the airport, in trucks and armored vehicles, and have a base in Tabarre near the US Embassy (EFE, AP via Africanews, Rezo Nodwes). Parrain Infos says a Salvadoran commando reached Claudy Célestin's house in Santo but he escaped. Counts: 2,400 to 2,800 (Rolfy Martínez, no source), more than 2,000 (EFE, RFI). Le Quotidien 509 notes that decree 679 lets the contingent work from the Dominican Republic, renews itself automatically and bars handing any soldier to an international court or a foreign state. RFI found Port-au-Prince residents mostly welcoming, but asking how long the troops will stay.
    Map: Port-au-Prince (18.547°N, 72.340°W) (city center) · Tabarre (18.562°N, 72.251°W)
  • 31 Oct: Axios reports Bukele called Trump on Tuesday and was told "Do what you have to do"; up to 2,000 troops, a number that "could double or triple," under a bilateral agreement Haiti approved Wednesday, working alongside the Gang Suppression Force and Vectus. Counts now run to about 2,500 (Theti Mapping, Corvington, no source) and 2,577 (Parrain Infos, no source). Listín Diario saw seven helicopters (five MD500Es, two Black Hawks) refuel and arm at Jimaní on 29 Oct before flying to Haiti; Johnston says personnel flights are hidden from public trackers. Bukele says the troops leave the day the four are returned alive, and otherwise "we will finish them off" along the way (Diario El Mundo). UNA-Haiti, a diaspora group, accuses the Salvadorans of air strikes on civilian areas since 22 Oct; no outlet on the ground reports such strikes.
  • 30 Oct: Bukele told Haitians in Creole the deployment is "not an operation against the Haitian people, nor an invasion," and (per Jake Johnston) that El Salvador pays 90 percent of the cost. The Miami Herald says the contingent grew from a 125-member aviation unit to at least 2,000 and was expected to reach about 2,500 by the end of Thursday, more than the whole Gang Suppression Force (2,526); most have not had the force's human rights course, no status-of-forces agreement has been signed, and the troops camp near the US Embassy. Helicopters: five seen at the El Rodeo fort in Jimaní, Dominican Republic (César Moreno video), and five to seven over the Cul-de-Sac plain (Rolfy Martínez video, Radio Télé Confiance). Charters from Honduras and Ecuador and Colombian airlifts continue. HaitiInfoProj says the goal is 5,000 (no source named). Opinion in El Salvador is divided (CNN en Español).
  • 29 Oct: Bukele and Prime Minister Fils-Aimé spoke by phone on the evening of 28 Oct. El Salvador's foreign ministry speaks of "more than 2,000 soldiers"; the Haitian communiqué says the two will deepen cooperation "within the framework" of the Gang Suppression Force. On 28 Oct Le Nouvelliste's editorial called it cooperation, "not an invasion," and described an air bridge of cargo and troop flights; EFE says the deployment runs outside the Security Council's September 2025 mandate. Jake Johnston expects at least 3,000 troops and asks who pays. Counts differ (1,300, 1,712, 1,962, 2,077). Accounts without named sources say the first clash with Chen Mechan and 400 Mawozo came on the night of 27 to 28 Oct (8 wounded, or 8 killed, depending on the account) and that the two leaders' houses in Croix-des-Bouquets were destroyed.
    Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W)
  • 27 to 28 Oct: about 1,000 Salvadoran soldiers have now deployed, says a security official in Haiti who was not named (Reuters); who commands them and how long they stay is unclear. The flights go on: a second charter on Tuesday (Jacqueline Charles), more cargo flights (Jake Johnston, who asks who is paying), the first Colombian Air Force C-130H from San Salvador (@LatAmMilMVMTs), US Southern Command C-130s (an unnamed US military official, via Reuters) and Ecuador's Aeroregional airline (El Español). The FAA says Haiti's airspace is not closed.
  • Opinion: CID Gallup (24 to 27 Oct) finds 94 percent of Salvadorans approve of Bukele and 82 percent worry about the risk to personnel in Haiti. OAS Secretary General Albert Ramdin expressed solidarity and called for more decisive action against the gangs (via Magazine Haïti). Haitian commentator Cyrus Sibert calls for a Haiti–El Salvador defense agreement; the Haitian group KONTRAPÈPLA calls the deployment a giveaway of sovereignty.
  • Dominican Republic, 27 Oct: Haiti's foreign ministry summoned Ambassador Faruk Miguel over reported Dominican army incursions (letter photo posted by Franklin Vargas); Diario Libre's unnamed source sets the meeting for Thursday 29 Oct at noon, and an unofficial Dominican version says the houses involved were on the Dominican side. Empire Média says soldiers entered Thomazeau and destroyed homes. El Guayabo's district head says armed Haitians crossed into Elías Piña carrying wounded (Proceso.com.do). The executive sent the Senate a bill with up to 30 years in prison for recruiting minors into armed groups.
    Map: Thomazeau (18.652°N, 72.094°W)
  • 26 to 27 Oct: the flights continue. The Miami Herald counts at least a dozen since Thursday, two on Sunday and cargo flights on Monday. Figures differ by source: 450 by Saturday "with hundreds more expected" (Herald), more than 500 special forces (Washington Post), at least 1,000 may ultimately deploy (Jake Johnston, no source given).
  • A separate command: Johnston (no source) and the Miami Herald ("reportedly") say Bukele wants some Salvadoran troops to operate outside the GSF, answering to his own commander in Haiti. Johnston: "A multilateral force, a bilateral force, and a private force."
  • The remains: a person familiar with the matter told the Washington Post it is "fairly certain" at least some are not the Salvadorans'; roughly three bodies were "almost fused together," and DNA testing is being done outside Haiti. Bukele has posted nothing on X since Sunday night, and Diario El Mundo says his government has given no new information nine days on.
  • How the four ended up there: Diario El Mundo says they used Google Maps to reach a supermarket and entered a gang area by mistake (no source named); the Washington Post says they "apparently lost their way"; analyst Romain Le Cour (El País) asks why four soldiers were in an unescorted vehicle in a gang-held area.
  • A video shared about 138,000 times as Bukele's commandos raiding a gang house in Haiti is from Ecuador, according to César Moreno; the posting account gave no source.
  • Dominican Republic, 26 Oct: Abinader says he backs Bukele, would receive him, and "will not allow those gangs within 20 kilometers of the border"; he says Haitian authorities themselves do not know the four's status. The foreign ministry says a post that had Bukele attacking the Dominican government is fake.
  • Remains, 25 Oct: the Gang Suppression Force says it received, through a humanitarian group, remains "suspected" to be the four, but "is not in a position to confirm" it. Bukele says they are not his men. The remains, recovered by the US-funded group Hero Client Rescue (Scott Reece), came with two military badges and a shirt and were badly burned (Miami Herald, Haitian Times). Vant Bèf Info says identification continues using dental records.
  • The Chadian ID: Bukele posted a Chadian GSF soldier's ID as found among the remains. The GSF says the card was lost during the search and the soldier is with his unit; AyiboPost and Vant Bèf Info name him as Sossal Kamis Alhadj and say he was wounded but is alive. After Haití en Español wrote that Bukele had called the soldier dead and then corrected itself, Bukele answered: "A little reading comprehension next time wouldn't hurt." Jake Johnston: "I can't imagine the GSF is thrilled about this."
  • Lanmò San Jou's 25 Oct TikTok video says the four died in Chen Mechan's area "by mistake" and that he would have taken them alive and negotiated with Bukele (Rolfy Martínez, Le Patrimoine 509). The New York Times says local media reported Chen Mechan and 400 Mawozo killed and burned them; no ransom demand has been made.
  • Four Salvadoran aviation technicians serving with the Gang Suppression Force went missing on 18 Oct 2026 after their armored vehicle was attacked in Santo, Croix-des-Bouquets (Haitian Times; Salvadoran military via AP). Bukele says they are medical-evacuation personnel, not combatants.
    Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W)
  • On 21 Oct Bukele announced a military operation to rescue them and warned the gangs in Haitian Creole that he could "finish" them as he did in El Salvador.
  • Remains: on 24 Oct a group called HERO handed burned remains, collected at a drop site in Chen Mechan territory, to the Gang Suppression Force, while saying it was "in no way claiming it is them" (Jacqueline Charles, Miami Herald). The New York Times (Frances Robles) reported bodies "believed to be" the four. Bukele: "They are not our men"; he says the remains are a child, a much taller man and someone in part of another army's uniform, with other countries' ID cards and uniforms. Jake Johnston notes there is no official confirmation and the remains need an autopsy.
  • First combat: Franklin Vargas reports the first clash in the Cul-de-Sac plain with at least one Salvadoran wounded; Latinar says four were wounded. El Demócrata reports fighting against 400 Mawozo in Croix-des-Bouquets.
    Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W)
  • Troop numbers differ by source; see the reference table at the bottom of the page. Johnston, from flight listings, counted charter flights from Washington and from El Salvador into Haiti on 23 October, one likely a US Air Force plane.
  • Law: El Salvador's Special Transitory Law for the participation of its armed forces in the Gang Suppression Force was approved 29 Sep, published 30 Sep, in force 2 Oct 2026, and authorizes participation until Oct 2027 (Diario El Mundo, El Salvador).
  • Equipment: Armapedia's and Premium Media's photos show Yagu armored vehicles loaded onto a US C-130J at Comalapa; Menoni's and Premium Media's videos show armored vehicles at Las Américas airport in the Dominican Republic, which Premium Media says are bound for Port-au-Prince. Rolfy Martínez posted video he says shows "P50 tanks" in Port-au-Prince (not yet reported by others).
    Map: Port-au-Prince (18.547°N, 72.340°W) (city center)
  • Dominican Republic: President Luis Abinader says he spoke with Bukele about logistical and intelligence cooperation against the gangs, and that he has reinforced the border with soldiers, armored vehicles, drones and aircraft. Listín Diario's reporter in Jimaní saw an army contingent arrive while the Mal Paso crossing stayed open.
  • On 25 Oct Abinader said two more battalions, heavy equipment and artillery have gone to the southern border, on top of about 11,000 men (Diario Libre). Listín Diario says the government has still not explained how Dominican army and police ammunition reached 400 Mawozo and other gangs, as the UN Panel of Experts reported.

The Gang Suppression Force (UN-authorized)

  • 13 Nov: @GSForceHaiti reports an early-morning raid in Cité Soleil on 12 Nov: one suspected gang member killed, two injured, others fled with the bodies; firearms, ammunition, Molotov cocktails and marijuana seized (video, 3 photos; gang not named). The prime minister, visiting the Téléco base in Kenscoff, cited the force's support. Chad, which has soldiers in the force, agreed with Haiti in Paris to strengthen security cooperation. GCR2P says the UN has indicated most of the extra Salvadoran troops operate outside the force's mandate and monitoring; AFP (France 24) says the force distances itself from them. No new BINUH post on the force.
    Map: Cité Soleil (18.578°N, 72.335°W) · Kenscoff (18.450°N, 72.287°W)
  • 12 Nov: @GSForceHaiti posted its first operation since 7 Nov: north of the airport on 11 Nov, weapons and ammunition seized (video; no figures). Rolfy Martínez says the force and the army are in Martissant. BINUH says the force supported at least six police operations from September, against 400 Mawozo, Kraze Baryè, Village de Dieu, Gran Grif and Kokorat San Ras, and ran two of its own. The G7 welcomed the mandate renewal through March 2027 and urged money for the UN trust fund. Lamothe proposes the force concentrate on the Artibonite and Mirebalais. No new BINUH post on the force.
    Map: Village de Dieu (18.538°N, 72.350°W) (neighborhood, approximate point) · Artibonite (19.337°N, 72.492°W) (department, approximate center) · Mirebalais (18.834°N, 72.105°W) · Martissant (18.527°N, 72.357°W) (neighborhood, approximate point)
  • 11 Nov: Axios says U.S. chargé d'affaires David Howell texted the prime minister on 4 Nov that the Salvadorans "nearly killed GSF troops" and asked him to have them stop until they coordinate; El Salvador says it never fired toward the force's Chadian soldiers on 3 Nov. Jake Johnston notes Rubio said there is "no plan B" if the force fails. The prime minister's post puts the force beside El Salvador: "Haiti, El Salvador, GSF: same fight." Abinader urges the force and the Salvadorans to "come to an agreement and coordinate." No new post from @GSForceHaiti since 7 Nov; no new post from BINUH (@BINUH_UN) since the last run (AlterPresse carried its call for women's part in the elections).
  • 10 Nov: spokesman Igor Rugwiza, quoted by Rolfy Martínez, says the force "did not take part in any joint operation" on the night of 7 to 8 Nov. Its commander, Maj. Gen. Erdenebat Batsuuri, met Defense Minister Andrésol and police chief Paraison on 9 Nov on dividing the ground and guarding strategic sites. Jake Johnston says Rubio warned of the force and Salvadorans firing on each other "because it's already happened" (no details; not yet reported by others) and notes the force is paid for by UN assessed contributions through UNSOH. No new post from @GSForceHaiti since 7 Nov. BINUH posted only on women in the elections.
  • 9 Nov: no new post from @GSForceHaiti since 7 Nov. Vant Bèf Info names the force among those in the strike on Lanmò San Jou's house; the force said on 7 Nov that it uses no explosive drones. Rubio says the force and the Salvadoran troops need "a deconfliction mechanism" so they do not shoot at each other. BINUH (@BINUH_UN) posted only an item on first-time voters; its head, in an AyiboPost interview, says the authorities decided not to negotiate with gangs.
  • 8 Nov: @GSForceHaiti posted again on 7 Nov, its first post since 1 Nov: spokesman Igor Rugwiza says some Salvadorans will join the force under its command and rules of engagement while others stay under Salvadoran command in a bilateral framework; the mission is not limited to Croix-des-Bouquets; and "the GSF does not use explosive drones," including from its helicopters (video; HaitiInfoProj transcript; Infobae). The Miami Herald says the force was not part of the Mathias drone strike, often has to de-conflict when it learns of other operations, and has increased its presence in the capital; Pirate Wire Services says the police credited the GSF. AyiboPost cites a UN source that only 200 of the new Salvadorans are under the force and says at least four force soldiers have been wounded in these operations.
    Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W)
  • 7 Nov: @GSForceHaiti has still posted nothing since 1 Nov. Johnston says reports of Salvadorans "saving" the force the other night were fake, while separately confirming wounded Salvadorans. Infobae / Le Cour Grandmaison again counts the force as one of five structures with no shared plan. Radio Télé Confiance and Le Concret include the force among units in the 5 Nov Cité Soleil drone push that killed Mathias (unnamed sources); the force itself has not said so.
    Map: Cité Soleil (18.578°N, 72.335°W)
  • 6 Nov: Ulloa cites help to a Chadian force under attack as a result of the bilateral deployment; @GSForceHaiti has still posted nothing since 1 Nov. Jake Johnston posts "There is no peacekeeping mission in Haiti." The UN line that only about 200 Salvadorans fall under the force is repeated in Al Jazeera's 5 Nov wrap.
  • 5 Nov: the police say the force took part in the Santo operation that found the burned vehicle and in a Laplèn operation (police posts of 4 Nov); @GSForceHaiti has still posted nothing since 1 Nov. El Salvador's foreign minister says it stays in the force; Edwin Paraison suggests the other Salvadoran troops formally join it. Mario Nawfal writes that "UN-backed forces lag behind" the Salvadorans.
  • 4 Nov: Chadian troops of the force came under gang attack in Croix-des-Bouquets and Tabarre on the evening of 3 Nov and Salvadoran helicopters came to their aid (Bukele; EFE cites a force report); @GSForceHaiti has posted nothing since 1 Nov. At the UN, Dujarric said about 200 Salvadorans fall under the force and the rest are "a bilateral issue"; the Miami Herald says the Salvadorans skipped UN human rights training and no signed agreement is public. The prime minister says the forces now pass 4,000; the force's deputy commander, Brig. Elon Clarke of Jamaica, stood with him (Haiti Defense Observer).
    Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W) · Tabarre (18.562°N, 72.251°W)
  • 3 Nov: the police said they launched the 2 Nov Cul-de-Sac operation "with the support of" the force, at Kafou Chada, the Croix-des-Missions bridge and Santo 17; the police statement does not mention the Salvadorans (Centre à la Une). No new post from @GSForceHaiti since 1 Nov. Planet News HT recalls the force's 1 Nov statement that it uses no explosive devices, as Haitian outlets report kamikaze drones at Santo.
  • 2 Nov: a ship left the Dominican port of Haina on 1 Nov with about 525 tonnes of weapons, vehicles and support gear from Bangladesh, moved with German funding, for the force; UNSOH head Daniela Kroslak called Dominican help "essential" (Dominican foreign ministry via Diario Libre, Listín Diario, El Diario de Hoy, Le Quotidien 509). Diario Libre says the gear serves the Bangladeshi contingent of about 1,600 soldiers and police. Le Quotidien 509 says the force's command structure and coordination are still being worked out. Another source says more than 600 tonnes (Le Nouvelliste, 30 Oct; HaitiBoiii). The force posted nothing new overnight.
  • 1 Nov: the force said it "does not use explosive devices in its operations," naming "the Salvadoran contingent and aircraft operating under the GSF," and called any suggestion otherwise "without foundation" (not yet reported by others). The statement treats the Salvadoran contingent as part of the force. Le Quotidien 509 says the force told it that it gives no figures. Drop Site counts at least 78 Salvadorans in the force since early 2025.
  • 31 Oct: the US thanked Germany for airlifting more than 600 tonnes of priority equipment from Bangladesh for the force. Axios says the Salvadorans work alongside it. Rolfy Martínez credits the force, with the police and El Salvador, in the 29 Oct Bercy operation; other outlets do not mention it.
  • 30 Oct: the EU welcomed the Security Council's extension of the force's mandate (to 31 Mar 2027) and called for credible elections, without mentioning El Salvador. Amitié FM relays the force's warning that "disinformation can put lives in danger." Kirikounews says Chadian forces quarreled with the Salvadoran defense minister and refuse to help the Salvadorans (no source named; not yet reported by others). Johnston says El Salvador's statement does not even acknowledge the force, and Le Cour notes a single national deployment now outnumbers it.
  • 28 Oct: the force says it will keep up the pressure on the gangs (Le Quotidien 509). Its own X account has posted nothing since the 28 Oct 01:00 statement on false information. The Dominican Republic says it has spent more than $21 million supporting the force.
  • 27 Oct statement: the force accuses the gangs of spreading false information to undermine its operations and asks people to rely on official channels. Reuters puts the force at about 2,000, a third of its planned strength. EFE says the press is kept away from Toussaint Louverture airport and names the commander as Maj. Gen. Erdenebat Batsuuri. Rolfy Martínez posted video of the force sweeping Ganthier, Croix-des-Bouquets.
    Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W) · Ganthier (18.532°N, 72.068°W)
  • 26 Oct statement: "Pressure on criminal groups will continue, and their acts of violence will not prevent the GSF from carrying out its mandate." EFE describes the photos: a convoy of eleven armored vehicles, Chadian soldiers, and a soldier in a Salvadoran helmet flying over the capital.
  • Size, per Dominican Foreign Minister Víctor "Ito" Bisonó (26 Oct): about 2,181 international personnel deployed, with a goal of about 5,500 within three months. The Dominican Republic gives the force logistics (transit, medical evacuations, air permits, blood, military cargo) but takes no direct part in operations.
  • Operations, 26 Oct: Salvadoran troops under the GSF and Haitian police ran a joint operation in Bon Repos (Miami Herald); Rolfy Martínez reports police and GSF offensives in Marin and Lizon and posts video of armored vehicles in Kenscoff; residents tell him the GSF presence in Croix-des-Bouquets is heavy.
    Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W) · Kenscoff (18.450°N, 72.287°W)
  • PassBlue says the US plan for the GSF is not going well; Johnston: "Hard to imagine Bukele is going to undermine the GSF unless he's coordinating with at least some in USG."
  • 25 Oct statement: received remains through "a third-party humanitarian organization"; cannot yet say they are the missing Salvadorans; the Chadian ID card belongs to a living GSF soldier; coordination with Haitian security forces "remains essential." Special representative Jack Christofides sent condolences to the families and to El Salvador (Haitian Times, EFE).
  • Size: over 2,000 troops from six countries (Miami Herald); another source (EFE) says more than 1,000, from El Salvador, Guatemala, Jamaica, Mongolia, Sri Lanka and Chad.
  • Kenya's foreign affairs principal secretary, Korir Sing'oei, says he is "deeply concerned" at the danger to GSF personnel: "We stand in solidarity with the GSF."
  • The Gang Suppression Force (GSF) is the UN-authorized force under Security Council resolution 2793 (2025). Its mandate was renewed in September 2026 for six months, through March 2027 (Haitian Times).
  • Its commander is Mongolian Maj. Gen. Erdenebat Batsuuri (Haitian Times). Contributing countries named by Haïti Liberté: Chad, El Salvador, Guatemala, Jamaica, Mongolia, Sri Lanka and Bangladesh. The UN calls it severely understrength; EFE says it has about 2,000 of a planned 5,500 from six countries.
  • In July a GSF patrol in Tabarre came under fire near an illegal checkpoint in Kraze Baryè territory; the GSF said three armed men were killed (Haitian Times, 19 Oct).
    Map: Tabarre (18.562°N, 72.251°W)

Haitian police and army

  • 13 Nov: the police say they and the Task Force took "total control" of the Sylvio Cator stadium and nearby areas early on 12 Nov, then handed it to the Salvadorans; no toll given. Haiti Defense Observer says the army had been inside since 10 Nov and posts AP photos of Haitian and Salvadoran soldiers at Portail Léogâne, next to Village de Dieu. The prime minister visited the Téléco forward base in Kenscoff with the justice minister and EU and OAS representatives; the police posted a visit to their "first FOB." Jacqueline Charles says sources tell her the police had the location of Lanmò San Jou's house as recently as summer 2025. The CNDDR set up departmental disarmament networks; former senator Gracia Delva (under Canadian sanctions) asks for operations in Marchand-Dessalines.
    Map: Village de Dieu (18.538°N, 72.350°W) (neighborhood, approximate point) · Dessalines (19.261°N, 72.517°W) · Kenscoff (18.450°N, 72.287°W) · Léogâne (18.511°N, 72.633°W)
  • 12 Nov: the army took over the Sylvio Cator stadium downtown and its engineers cleared bus barricades in Martissant; new Guardian Xtreme armored vehicles were seen downtown (Haiti Defense Observer). Police in the Nippes added checkpoints and patrols to keep out gang members fleeing the capital; police posted road clearing in Gressier. BINUH counts 44 extrajudicial executions involving police from July to September (14 the quarter before) and 165 gang members, 24 of them boys, killed by police in Gressier from 12 Aug to 30 Sep. The Miami Herald says at least $7 million paid for police rifles and ammunition is missing; Justice Minister Pélissier is pursuing Fencebridge.
    Map: Martissant (18.527°N, 72.357°W) (neighborhood, approximate point) · Gressier (18.543°N, 72.523°W)
  • 11 Nov: the police and the Task Force pushed into Martissant 2A and Sainte-Bernadette on 10 Nov with armored vehicles and kamikaze drones (Radio Télé Confiance, Innova News); Magazine Haïti calls it the first operation at the capital's southern entrance in years; no results given. The Arcahaie mayor says two police helicopters stopped three boats of gunmen on 7 Nov, counts 24 dead and 1,898 families displaced, and asks for an operation at Trou Forban and Bercy. A suspected 400 Mawozo member was lynched in Arcahaie. Defense Minister Andrésol visited army positions in Gressier; the prime minister and police chief Paraison visited Kenscoff. Fils-Aimé praises Bukele in public and says a partnership agreement was signed about three weeks ago (not published). Chérizier, in a video relayed by Rolfy Martínez, accuses the prime minister of giving Krisla weapons, offering no evidence.
    Map: Martissant (18.527°N, 72.357°W) (neighborhood, approximate point) · Kenscoff (18.450°N, 72.287°W) · Arcahaie (18.770°N, 72.512°W) · Gressier (18.543°N, 72.523°W)
  • 10 Nov: the Miami Herald says Haitian police officers fly the Task Force drones that hit Lanmò San Jou's house; no police statement on the strike. The Defense Ministry says it will coordinate operations with the police and the Gang Suppression Force. 114 Haitian army recruits are in basic training in Huehuetoca, Mexico (iciHaiti; Mexico's Defense Ministry). An opposition coalition demands the prime minister's exit and accuses the government of gang links without proof; marchers echo the call.
  • 9 Nov: the police communiqué dated 8 Nov says Mathias Saintil was hit by "several bullets" in an operation on 5 Nov between 8 and 11 p.m. near the Aciérie d'Haïti and died on 6 Nov; it does not mention drones. Police and the Task Force fired drones at kidnapping hideouts in Bel-Air and Delmas 2 on 8 Nov (Innova News, Le Relief). Cerca-Carvajal police arrested two men on National Road 3, one linked to Izo 5 Segond. The army posted video of "consolidation operations" in Kenscoff. "Gang hunter" prosecutor Jean Ernest Muscadin quit to run for president.
    Map: Kenscoff (18.450°N, 72.287°W) · Bel-Air (18.550°N, 72.336°W) (neighborhood, approximate point) · Delmas (18.552°N, 72.292°W)
  • 8 Nov: the police posted that "the PNH and other forces neutralized a big terrorist gang chief" and say Mathias died of gunshots; RNDDH says a kamikaze drone hit him at Nan Nélio and lists at least seven drones on 5 Nov in Cité Soleil, Tabarre, Croix-des-Missions, Santo and the Cul-de-Sac plain, with civilians killed at a Pierre-Six party (Miami Herald, FCN, Reuters). Heavy gunfire and explosions in Tabarre on 7 Nov, with Cité Doudoune targeted by drones (Vant Bèf Info; U.S. Embassy alert). Late on 7 Nov, gunfire near the downtown flour mill, panic in Village de Dieu and a drone blast downtown (Tag News via HaitiInfoProj). Armed men are digging up National Road 1 at Kafou Marin, La Plaine; a vigilante group burned houses in Pon Mate, Arcahaie, hunting "Pouchon" (both Tag News; not yet reported by others). The disarmament commission (CNDDR) urges gang members to give up their weapons but says there is no general amnesty. Hundreds rallied for Bukele in Canapé-Vert, Belladère and Jérémie (Haitian Times). Haiti Defense Observer says army soldiers are embedded in the Task Force. Cyrus Sibert and Jean Willio Patrick Chrispin argue opposite sides on foreign help.
    Map: Village de Dieu (18.538°N, 72.350°W) (neighborhood, approximate point) · Cité Soleil (18.578°N, 72.335°W) · Belladère (18.866°N, 71.764°W) · Arcahaie (18.770°N, 72.512°W) · Tabarre (18.562°N, 72.251°W)
  • 7 Nov: Prime Minister Fils-Aimé posts "Un chef de gang en moins"; Haitian outlets name Mathias Saintil of the Boston gang, Cité Soleil, killed after a 5 Nov police and Task Force drone strike (Magazine Haïti, Radio Télé Confiance, Le Concret, Le Médiateur; AP cites a local rights group). Police post a cleaning video on Boulevard 15 Octobre, reopened. Magazine Haïti reports thousands chanting "Viv Bukele" in Belladère and the West. 400 Mawozo tells AP it contacted the national disarmament commission.
    Map: Cité Soleil (18.578°N, 72.335°W) · Belladère (18.866°N, 71.764°W)
  • 6 Nov: Haitian army (FAd'H) armored trucks are filmed carrying Salvadoran troops from Clercine (Haiti Defense Observer; Rolfy Martínez). Rolfy posts Kenscoff residents returning to work without gang tolls after a joint police–Salvadoran push; Le Midi Info (3 Nov) already had the army consolidating there. FJKL asks the government to break the private-contractor contract and train Haitian soldiers with Salvadoran help (RHINEWS, L'Observatoire, Zantray).
    Map: Kenscoff (18.450°N, 72.287°W)
  • 5 Nov: the police say residents of Fisi and Kablen are going home and Gressier residents are being escorted back to work (videos); the western department police chief visited Kenscoff, where the army says it is consolidating (Radio Télé Confiance). Arcahaie's mayor puts the 31 Oct to 2 Nov attacks at 16 dead, civilians and brigade members, with a mass grave and 26 houses burned (Le Nouvelliste); Vant Bèf Info calls it revenge for the police killing of "Galaxy." France pledged training and equipment for the police and army (Forbin and Éléonore Caroit in Madrid). Haiti Defense Observer says there are more Salvadoran soldiers in Haiti than Haitian ones. The national brewery BRANA has shut for now; Zanmi Lasante reports damage at its Santo site; gunfire stopped a 2 Nov memorial Mass at Croix-des-Missions (Infos Partage video). Le Midi Info says the Cité Soleil town hall may change after the UN experts' report (no source named). DEBLOKE denies that Martelly asked the UN to stop the Salvadoran operations (Juno7).
    Map: Cité Soleil (18.578°N, 72.335°W) · Kenscoff (18.450°N, 72.287°W) · Arcahaie (18.770°N, 72.512°W) · Gressier (18.543°N, 72.523°W)
  • 4 Nov: at the Villa d'Accueil on 3 Nov, Prime Minister Fils-Aimé said the police, the army and the force share one plan, "the gangs will get no sleep," and both "gangs in sandals" and "gangs in ties" will face sanctions; his communiqué says the state is determined to crush ("écraser") the gangs, lists 3,258 new police officers and armored vehicles on order, and says operations will spread from Kenscoff and Gressier to the West, Artibonite and Centre. Radio Télé Confiance and Corvington Matin doubt it, recalling that on Friday he said security cannot depend on foreign forces. In Arcahaie the interim mayor says at least 22 hostages were freed at Bercy on 2 Nov and some people were burned in their houses; Radio Télé Confiance says the police and brigades retook the road from Thomas to Bercy and freed about 22 Full Gospel church members. The army says it is consolidating in Kenscoff, with foot patrols on 3 Nov; army chief Lt. Gen. Derby Guerrier is in Benin from 3 to 7 Nov. Chérizier urged the FBI to look at the gang leader "Ezekiel" and posted a video he says was made "especially for the FBI."
    Map: Artibonite (19.337°N, 72.492°W) (department, approximate center) · Kenscoff (18.450°N, 72.287°W) · Arcahaie (18.770°N, 72.512°W) · Gressier (18.543°N, 72.523°W)
  • 3 Nov: the police announced a big operation in La Plaine to destroy illegal toll barriers and stop kidnappings (police statement via Radio Métronome). Magazine Haïti says the police are also fighting at Bèsi in Arcahaie, where "more than 20 bandits" fell; Arcahaie residents say the fighting is in its fourth day and Viv Ansanm holds part of the town. Several thousand people marched in Canaan against the Salvadorans, under gang pressure according to Magazine Haïti, Amerique.info7 and Centre à la Une; the police broke up the march. RHAJAC accuses Michel Martelly of being behind the 28 Oct UNA-Haiti letter, without public evidence. In the Moïse trial in Miami, a witness said a police officer at the residence told the attackers the gate would be left open.
    Map: Arcahaie (18.770°N, 72.512°W)
  • 2 Nov: in Arcahaie, a local toll attributed to town officials gives 43 dead, 13 of them civilians, and 18 wounded (Rezo Nodwes); another source says more than 30 dead on 1 Nov at La Vigne and Pon Toman (Centre à la Une), and Radio Métronome gave at least seven on 1 Nov. The police say they are holding with the BOID and CIMO units after pushing back a 2 a.m. attack at Bési on 31 Oct; residents say "between the police statement and the reality on the ground there is a gap" (Corvington Matin), and Ricnews says the gangs keep getting reinforcements. Mayor René Wilner asked for helicopters and kamikaze drones. Prime Minister Fils-Aimé told the 37th police class that "the security of the country cannot depend on foreign forces." The police union SPNH-17 says more than 60 percent of officers guard officials and business figures. Defense Minister Mario Andrésol spoke on army doctrine in a YouTube interview (Haiti Defense Observer). The Dominican Republic stepped up watch at Jimaní and Pedernales (Listín Diario).
    Map: Arcahaie (18.770°N, 72.512°W)
  • 1 Nov: gangs from Cabaret, reinforced from Kenscoff (the mayor) and named as the "Taliban" gang by Vant Bèf Info, attacked Vigner, Thoman and Sou Rigòl in Arcahaie from about 2:45 a.m. on 31 Oct, burning houses and vehicles; Amerique.info7 (via Descargador Miniaturas) says 12 civilians were killed. The Arcahaie police and the local self-defense brigade, with "auxiliary security personnel," hit back overnight; the attackers pulled back and barricades were cleared (HaitiInfoProj), and Infos Partege video shows gang corpses dragged through town. A brigade member spoke of "mixed signals from the government in the capital." The mayor asked the Task Force to step in. The police held a minute of silence for the four Salvadorans, led by the prime minister (RT en Español). Six 400 Mawozo members reportedly surrendered at the Clercine police station (Parrain Infos, citing journalist Guerrier Henry). Phénil Gordon Désir was sent to the United States on 31 Oct (Magazine Haïti; Corvington Matin says 30 Oct). Corvington Matin says the government still tells the public nothing about the Salvadoran forces.
    Map: Kenscoff (18.450°N, 72.287°W) · Arcahaie (18.770°N, 72.512°W) · Cabaret (18.734°N, 72.418°W)
  • 31 Oct: the police, backed by the Task Force (Le Nouvelliste; Magazine Haïti, citing the Arcahaie mayor, says by kamikaze drones), killed the Cabaret gang leader "Galaxie" and about ten of his men at Bercy, between Cabaret and Arcahaie, on 29 Oct; others fled toward Lévêque. The 37th police class graduated on 30 Oct: 1,189 officers, 242 of them women (Prime Minister's office; the police had said 1,192). 400 Mawozo and Chen Mechan blocked the roads of Rémy with rocks (HaitiInfoProj, no source). A 36th-class officer was shot dead on Nazon. The judicial police called for Phénil Gordon Désir in the Moïse case. The government and UNDP plan to retake downtown Port-au-Prince (Champ-de-Mars, then Parliament from Jan 2027). Canada says sanctioned people who fund gangs have no place in the elections.
    Map: Port-au-Prince (18.547°N, 72.340°W) (city center) · Arcahaie (18.770°N, 72.512°W) · Cabaret (18.734°N, 72.418°W)
  • 30 Oct: the police graduated its 37th class, 1,192 officers. In the lower Artibonite a joint BRI and Gang Suppression Force operation on 28 Oct reportedly killed more than ten suspected Gran Grif members (Dark_Linkz, no source named), and Jean Aladin of a self-defense brigade was arrested on 27 Oct over messages with a Gran Grif member (Bertide Horace via InnovaNews7). IOM says 13,912 people fled Petite Rivière and Verrettes from 19 to 26 Oct. The electoral council set elections for 18 Jul and 4 Oct 2027. The government has still not said who commands the Salvadorans (Corvington, Frantz Duval on RFI). The Dominican ambassador's noon meeting at the foreign ministry: no outcome found; in Santo Domingo, Vinicio Castillo called the summons "a hostile act."
    Map: Artibonite (19.337°N, 72.492°W) (department, approximate center) · Verrettes (19.051°N, 72.467°W)
  • 28 to 29 Oct: police video of an advance in Gressier; Rolfy Martínez video of forces opening gang-blocked streets in Carrefour-Feuilles. MSF treated 30 people hurt by gunfire or drones from 18 to 26 Oct in Tabarre and Cité Soleil and warns of more casualties as foreign forces arrive. Journalist Richard Alexandre "Caonabo" Vedette was shot dead at Delmas 83 on 27 Oct. The foreign ministry has summoned the Dominican ambassador for noon on 29 Oct over the Thomazeau reports.
    Map: Cité Soleil (18.578°N, 72.335°W) · Carrefour (18.535°N, 72.409°W) · Thomazeau (18.652°N, 72.094°W) · Gressier (18.543°N, 72.523°W) · Tabarre (18.562°N, 72.251°W) · Delmas (18.552°N, 72.292°W)
  • 27 Oct: the government has still said nothing about the Salvadoran deployment (EFE); HaitiInfoProj calls it a press blackout. Foreign Minister Raina Forbin held a virtual meeting with the heads of Haiti's 57 missions abroad on the government's foreign-policy roadmap (Ayitian Avan News). The ministry summoned the Dominican ambassador (see the El Salvador section).
  • KONTRAPÈPLA, a citizens' group, in a note of 26 Oct signed by Jean William Jeanty, accuses the state of "liquidating" Haiti's sovereignty and ceding ground to El Salvador, criticizes Bukele's threats and the authorities' silence, and raises concerns about the Cul-de-Sac plain (Rezo Nodwes, Lune News).
  • The national disarmament commission (CNDDR), in a note dated 24 Oct, warned of violence and forced displacement in Santo and asked for operations that respect human rights and for seized weapons to be listed and handed over (Vant Bèf Info, 25 Oct).
  • Port-au-Prince, 27 Oct: people in the flooded Lalue displacement camps protested in the rain, burning tires and throwing stones (HaitiInfoProj). The electoral council has registered more than 878,000 voters, but the election calendar is uncertain (Radio Télé Confiance). Radio Télé Confiance also headlines that the ruling side's candidate sent large sums to gangs in Cité Soleil (candidate not named in the post).
    Map: Port-au-Prince (18.547°N, 72.340°W) (city center) · Cité Soleil (18.578°N, 72.335°W)
  • Bel-Air, Sunday night 25 Oct: a police operation that began around 5 p.m. used explosive drones (Le Médiateur says nearly five). Police sources tell Vant Bèf Info, Radio Télé Galaxie (via EFE) and HaitiInfoProj that several gang members were killed; no official toll. A resident (via HaitiInfoProj) says a local second in command called "Solo" was shot, and that innocent people were hurt by a drone strike.
    Map: Bel-Air (18.550°N, 72.336°W) (neighborhood, approximate point)
  • The police posted video of its BRI unit on the ground in Mirebalais (27 Oct).
    Map: Mirebalais (18.834°N, 72.105°W)
  • The Haitian army (FAd'H) tested about 2,000 recruits on Sunday for 700 places, part of a plan to recruit 15,000 in three years (EFE).
  • The Court of Appeal sent businessman Marc Antoine Acra and four others to trial without a jury in the 2015 Manzanares drug case (Radio Télé Confiance).
  • The Haitian Network of Anti-Corruption Journalists (RHAJAC) accuses Prime Minister Fils-Aimé of a quiet deal with gang leaders and asks for independent inquiries into who funds the gangs; it gives no evidence (Rezo Nodwes, EFE). In Gonaïves, the group COMOGINDA plans protests from November demanding operations against the gangs (Radio Tandans).
    Map: Gonaïves (19.446°N, 72.688°W)
  • Jake Johnston and Vant Bèf Info: the Haitian government still has said nothing publicly. Dominican Defense Minister Fernández Onofre says the gangs control at least 70 percent of Port-au-Prince.
    Map: Port-au-Prince (18.547°N, 72.340°W) (city center)
  • Sunday night, 25 Oct: forces in downtown Port-au-Prince carried out kamikaze drone strikes in Bel-Air, followed by fighting between PNH special units and gangs; PNH special units also entered gang-held parts of Mirebalais (Radio Télé Confiance, no casualty figures; not yet reported by others). The PNH posted video of its work to restore security in Petite Rivière de l'Artibonite.
    Map: Port-au-Prince (18.547°N, 72.340°W) (city center) · Artibonite (19.337°N, 72.492°W) (department, approximate center) · Mirebalais (18.834°N, 72.105°W) · Bel-Air (18.550°N, 72.336°W) (neighborhood, approximate point)
  • Gangs are blocking roads in the Cul-de-Sac plain: both bridges at Shada and parts of Santo 11 since Saturday, with civilians held as human shields (Radio Télé Confiance); the Croix-des-Missions bridge since Saturday night, with welded metal poles (Thériel Thélus).
  • Thélus says forces had entered nearly 80 percent of Chen Mechan's strongholds but did not hold the Damien area, so the gang has regrouped.
  • The Haitian National Police say an offensive began 18 Oct in the Cul-de-Sac plain, in Croix-des-Missions, Santo, Carrefour Marassa and the Dyo and Las Vegas corridors, with many gang members killed and no count given (EFE via Diario El Mundo).
    Map: Carrefour (18.535°N, 72.409°W)
  • El Demócrata (Dominican outlet) reports Haitian police and the international force operating in Butte Boyer and Cité Doudoune on 24 Oct.
  • Retired Col. Himmler Rébu, a former Haitian army special forces commander, told EFE the Salvadorans "will not get results" because they do not know Haiti's ground, history and politics.
  • Haiti's government has called the soldiers' release a priority and says it is coordinating with the GSF (Haitian Times).
  • Haiti's government postponed the elections planned for December indefinitely in early October (EFE via France 24).

United States

  • 13 Nov: no new statement found from the U.S. Embassy or the State Department. RNDDH's Pierre Espérance says Washington asked for better coordination, not a halt, and calls reports otherwise disinformation that protects the gangs (Magazine Haïti); AlterPresse reports organizations denouncing U.S. pressure on the Salvadoran offensive (its page blocked us); ALBA Movimientos says Bukele acts "in coordination with Marco Rubio." Asked why Haitian police had not acted earlier, Jake Johnston says both governments took part in the decision and asks "why has the Haitian government decided to take these actions now?"
  • 12 Nov: the G7 statement, which the United States joined, asks "all security actors operating in Haiti to prioritize the protection of Haitian civilians" and notes concern about sanctioned people in the political process; Michel Martelly, under U.S. sanctions, has hired a lobbyist (AyiboPost, via Jake Johnston). The U.S. Embassy was closed 11 Nov for Veterans Day and posted nothing else. Johnston tells The New Humanitarian he finds it "hard to believe" El Salvador pays for the deployment alone. Former Rep. Mark Green helped broker the Fencebridge arms deal now short $7 million (Miami Herald).
  • 11 Nov: Axios publishes the State Department side of the dispute: Howell's 4 Nov text asking the prime minister to halt the Salvadoran operations, and a 5 Nov protest note from the U.S. embassy in San Salvador saying "Salvadoran small arms fire" hit the U.S. embassy in Port-au-Prince on 31 Oct, "nearly hitting U.S. personnel." A State Department official says three Salvadorans were hurt, one flown out; a U.S. official blames "a class of bureaucrats" for resisting Trump's wishes. Jake Johnston says the deployment exposes divisions between Trump, who gave Bukele the go-ahead, and Rubio's State Department. @USEmbassyHaiti posted only a meeting with the culture minister; Ambassador Waltz posted nothing on Haiti.
    Map: Port-au-Prince (18.547°N, 72.340°W) (city center)
  • 10 Nov: Infobae, citing the State Department transcript, says Rubio was asked whether Salvadoran soldiers and private security contractors weaken the Gang Suppression Force and answered that the risk exists only if the chain of command fails. Jake Johnston calls Rubio's "We're not actively involved" "not exactly a ringing endorsement." @USEmbassyHaiti reposted the force's 7 Nov question-and-answer video; Ambassador Waltz posted on UN budget cuts, not Haiti.
  • 9 Nov: Secretary of State Rubio, on video (El Salvador Times dates it 8 Nov): Bukele "called the president and made him aware of what he's doing... We're not actively involved," but the Gang Suppression Force and Salvadoran soldiers need "a deconfliction mechanism and strong coordination." El Salvador Times says the Pentagon and State Department are designing an emergency liaison channel (no source; not yet reported by others). No new post from @USEmbassyHaiti.
  • 8 Nov: the U.S. Embassy issued a 7 Nov security alert on "ongoing security operations in the vicinity of the U.S. Embassy in Tabarre. Heavy gunfire is reported." The Guardian says the Salvadorans are setting up a base near the embassy. A U.S. C-5 and C-130 landed at Comalapa, El Salvador, on 5 Nov with vehicles; no U.S. statement links the cargo to Haiti (El Testigo). No new Waltz (@USAmbUN) post on Haiti was found; @USEmbassyHaiti posted only a youth-program item besides the alert. Jake Johnston (CEPR) posted nothing original since the last run; he reposted Etienne Côté-Paluck's report on possible Boston successors.
    Map: Tabarre (18.562°N, 72.251°W)
  • 7 Nov: no new US Embassy or Waltz post on the Salvadoran operation found in this run. Jake Johnston (CEPR) reports at least two Salvadoran troops seriously wounded and says he confirmed it with multiple sources; he also says some circulating "Salvadorans saved the GSF" clips are fake. Erik Prince, a US contractor figure tied to Vectus, publicly praises the "HNP Task Force" for the Mathias strike.
  • 6 Nov: no new US Embassy or Waltz post on the Salvadoran operation found in this run. Jake Johnston (CEPR) warns of rising disinformation on Haiti since Bukele got involved and says there is no peacekeeping mission there.
  • 5 Nov: the Council on Foreign Relations' Daily Brief (4 Nov) says the United States backs a counter-gang offensive built around the force and "has indicated it plans to work with Vectus Global." No new post on the Salvadoran operation from the US Embassy (@USEmbassyHaiti; its latest is the 2 Nov closure notice) or from Ambassador Waltz (@USAmbUN) in this run.
  • 4 Nov: Ambassador Mike Waltz mocked the 400 Mawozo leader's request for UN mediation in a "bingo card" post; Magazine Haïti gives his words in French as: if gang leaders stopped attacking international forces, terrorizing the police and abusing Haitians, "maybe then we could talk about mediation." The Miami Herald recalls Trump's "You have to do what you have to do." No US Embassy post since 2 Nov and no State Department statement on the helicopter fighting were found in this run.
  • 3 Nov: no State Department or US Embassy statement on the Cul-de-Sac operation; the embassy was closed on 2 Nov for All Souls' Day. AP and EFE recall the US reward of up to $1 million for Lanmò San Jou and his US indictment over the 2021 missionary kidnapping. Lawyer Gordon Phenil Désir, handed over by Haiti, made his first appearance in a US court in the Moïse case (Jacqueline Charles).
  • 2 Nov: American Airlines resumed daily Miami to Cap-Haïtien flights on 1 Nov; the FAA ban on US carriers at Port-au-Prince "remains in effect" (Miami Herald). The US Embassy welcomed the flights. We found no State Department or Embassy statement on the Salvadoran operation in this run.
    Map: Port-au-Prince (18.547°N, 72.340°W) (city center) · Cap-Haïtien (19.759°N, 72.201°W)
  • 1 Nov: Trump, at Mar-a-Lago on Saturday: "I did speak to him. He said that he has a big problem with Haiti... They killed some of his people. I said, 'You're running your country. You have to do what you have to do.'" (White House Rapid Response video). It is the first time Trump himself has described the call Axios reported. We found no State Department or US Embassy statement on the Salvadoran operation in this run.
  • 31 Oct: Axios says Trump told Bukele "Do what you have to do," that Rubio wants Haiti "off the radar," and that US requests for drones and transport planes were turned down, with Colombia a possible go-between; Johnston says the US and Colombian militaries helped with logistics. The US Embassy says the P-4000 program (more than $25 million from INL) has trained more than 3,200 officers and aims for 4,000 by spring 2027; it thanked Germany for an airlift to the Gang Suppression Force. No State Department or US mission to the UN post on the Salvadoran operation (their 30 Oct posts were about the G20, Cuba and Western Sahara). Rep. Brian Mast: "I hope the mission is revenge, retribution and destruction of the gangs."
  • 30 Oct: the Miami Herald reports at least one airlift of Salvadoran troops by a US military aircraft. Still no State Department, US Embassy or US mission to the UN statement on the operation. Le Cour says the US role, "which supported and facilitated this deployment, also deserves scrutiny" (AP).
  • 28 to 29 Oct: no new State Department, US Embassy or US mission to the UN statement on the Salvadoran operation. Le Nouvelliste says Washington backs it quietly; Patriarche Radio repeats the Miami Herald report of a US C-130 flight carrying Salvadoran troops on 23 Oct. Jake Johnston asks whether Washington or the UN support office is paying. The FAA says flights rerouted around Haiti on 27 Oct were due to a Miami air traffic control issue, not a closure.
  • 27 Oct: an unnamed US military official told Reuters that Southern Command C-130s helped move the Salvadoran troops. No new statement was found from the State Department or the US mission to the UN on 27 Oct (their posts that day were about Cuba), and the UN noon briefing had no Haiti item.
  • HERO Client Rescue, the group that recovered the remains, has about $9.31 million in State Department INL awards according to USAspending, starting with a 12 Aug 2024 contract for medical services to the Haitian police (Le Quotidien 509, which asks who authorized the recovery).
  • At a 24 to 25 Oct campaign rally in Perry, Georgia, Trump again called Haiti one of the "shithouse countries" while attacking Sen. Jon Ossoff for backing a longer TPS for Haitians (Le Quotidien 509).
  • Radio X Inter (Gonaïves) says 33 armored vehicles that arrived on 23 Oct went 20 to the GSF and 13 to the Haitian police, with the US Embassy facilitating; not yet reported by others. El País says three of the first six aircraft to land were American.
    Map: Gonaïves (19.446°N, 72.688°W)
  • No new statement found on 26 Oct from the State Department or the US mission to the UN; a US Embassy post that day carried only a photo and a link, which we did not open.
  • The group that recovered the remains, Hero Client Rescue, is a US-funded medical evacuation service led by Scott Reece, a former State Department security official (New York Times, Haitian Times). No new US government statement was found on 25 Oct; the State Department did not answer the Miami Herald about US transport for Salvadoran troops.
  • Ambassador Mike Waltz briefed the UN Security Council on 19 Oct and the US mission publicly told gang bosses the GSF "is coming for you."
  • Pimentel says Bukele was in Washington on 22 to 23 Oct to meet Trump and is asking for satellite intelligence, drones, armed helicopters and UN permission for offensive force. We have not found an official readout; the White House gallery page for a Trump-Bukele meeting that we opened is dated July 2025.
  • Jake Johnston (24 Oct) says the US military is giving logistical support to the Salvadoran deployment, and calls it "not a 'rescue' operation" but the US "again outsourcing to El Salvador… This is an end around." Premium Media's photos show Salvadoran armored vehicles loaded on a US C-130J.
  • The US designated Viv Ansanm and Gran Grif as terrorist organizations on 2 May 2025 (State Department).

Special forces and private contractors hired by Haiti's government

  • 13 Nov: nothing new from Vectus itself. Checked 13 Nov: Erik Prince's (@realErikDPrince) 12 Nov "Off Leash" update covers Iran, Spain and hybrid war, nothing on Haiti; no post from @VectusGlobal; vectusglobal.com unchanged, with no mention of Haiti. The police name the Task Force as their partner in retaking the Sylvio Cator stadium; EFE (via Infobae) calls the Task Force an armed group serving the government and "led by the American businessman Erik Prince"; AFP (France 24) calls it "a private security group serving the government." Bukele lists it among the forces in the stadium operation. GCR2P lists "a private military contractor" among the actors whose command and accountability need clarifying; ACLED says most of the anti-gang operations since 22 Oct involved the police and the Task Force.

No post from @VectusGlobal; vectusglobal.com (checked 12 Nov) unchanged, no mention of Haiti. BINUH's quarterly report says a government task force of police and "personnel from a foreign private military company" carried out ground operations and explosive drone strikes; 14 people with no gang ties were killed or injured by drones from July to September, and BINUH knows of no investigation. Johnston says the house had been hit before by "local forces working with Erik Prince." In The New Humanitarian, Romain Le Cour Grandmaison warns of a "Bukele-Prince model"; Juan José Martínez d'Aubuisson says the two have talked several times but he cannot say whether they have agreements in Haiti.

Bel Air gang members told CNN "drones are pushing us out."

  • Haiti's government created a Task Force that uses armed drones and helicopters against gangs and is supported by private contractors from Vectus Global, the company of Erik Prince (AP and Human Rights Watch).
  • Human Rights Watch (10 Mar 2026): Haitian security forces and private contractors working with them carried out drone strikes; at least 1,243 people were killed in 141 operations between 1 Mar 2025 and 21 Jan 2026, including at least 43 adults reportedly not gang members and 17 children; 738 were injured.
  • Vectus Global's contract was reportedly extended by one year at an additional $52 million, with operations expanded into Bas-Artibonite (Le Placentin, 10 Oct 2026, and other reports; we have not seen the contract). Reuters reported in Aug 2025 that Prince planned to keep personnel in Haiti for 10 years; OPB reported nearly 200 personnel.
