The gangs: who they are, where they operate

Viv Ansanm ("Living Together")

13 Nov: Haitian outlets say coalition members had held the Sylvio Cator stadium since February 2024 (Gazette Haiti, Passion Info Plus); Radio Télé Confiance says its fighters are moving away from their centers of operation. César Moreno posts coordinates he says mark a meeting point and hideout of Baz Pilat, an allied group, in Bolosse near the stadium, with leader "Petit Joe" (map only; not yet reported by others). 12 Nov: Barbecue posted two images on TikTok after Bukele's video, captioned "Mesi Pierre Espérance ak SDP" (relayed by Rolfy Martínez). A commentator relayed by Martínez says a government commission met gangs last Sunday (no evidence; not yet reported by others). 11 Nov: coalition leader Jimmy "Barbecue" Chérizier, in a video relayed by Rolfy Martínez, says the prime minister should stand trial with Krisla. 10 Nov: Le Facteur Haïti says Lanmò San Jou fled to Carrefour before the strike on his house; NovaVOX says some gang leaders are becoming less visible or asking for dialogue. 9 Nov: Radio Télé Aquarelle says many coalition chiefs have gathered in Carrefour; Haiti Defense Observer says Carrefour would be hard to take without air and sea support. 8 Nov: the Miami Herald and Le Nouvelliste place the Boston gang of the late Mathias Saintil inside Viv Ansanm; the Herald says the gangs appear to be under pressure and some leaders are fleeing their strongholds. 7 Nov: Mathias Saintil of the Boston gang (Cité Soleil), part of the wider Port-au-Prince gang map, is killed after a police and Task Force drone strike (PM; Maghaiti; AP rights group). 400 Mawozo, inside the coalition, tells AP it wants talks and a pause in some ops. France 24 / FJKL: gangs "seem panicked." 6 Nov: FJKL calls the coalition a "terrorist group" and says its members cannot enjoy impunity (RHINEWS, Zantray). 5 Nov: Vant Bèf Info attributes the 31 Oct to 2 Nov Arcahaie attacks to the coalition and says fighters pushed out of Kenscoff moved there. 4 Nov: Chérizier posted a video he says was made "especially for the FBI" and urged the FBI to look at the gang leader "Ezekiel"; HaitiInfoProj reads this as cooperation with the FBI. A Viv Ansanm barricade stood in Croix-des-Bouquets on 3 Nov (Centre à la Une photo). 2 Nov: Haitian outlets say the coalition forced residents of La Plaine and Canaan to march against the "invasion" under threat of death; Arcahaie residents say its fighters hold part of the town after four days. 29 Oct: Chérizier has gone quiet since the surge, his supporters say (Danny Shaw video); @rfemassala says some gang leaders have called for surrender (no source). The coalition of the main Port-au-Prince gangs, formed in 2024 per El País (one source dates it September 2023). Jimmy "Barbecue" Chérizier is a leading figure and its spokesman. Designated a terrorist organization by the US on 2 May 2025. The Miami Herald's analyst says it had been fragmenting, and that this incident may restore its cohesion. Early on 28 Oct Chérizier released a long video calling the accusations against him a plot by politicians and paid journalists, naming Youri Latortue among others (Rolfy Martínez). source

Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W) · Port-au-Prince (18.547°N, 72.340°W) (city center) · Cité Soleil (18.578°N, 72.335°W) · Carrefour (18.535°N, 72.409°W) · Kenscoff (18.450°N, 72.287°W) · Arcahaie (18.770°N, 72.512°W)
400 Mawozo

13 Nov: a Salvadoran account (@notindeslv) says Lanmò San Jou said on a live broadcast that he left his house to protect his mother, not out of fear, and would face the Salvadorans (not yet reported by others). Port-au-Prince Post, citing two sources, says gunmen remain visible at Carrefour Marasa near the Famosa company and that "Chamo," described as his right-hand man, has taken over a house there. Jacqueline Charles says sources tell her police had the house's location as recently as summer 2025. 12 Nov: RFI geolocates Bukele's strike video to Thomazeau; a man from the gang's strongholds held a press conference calling Lanmò San Jou an honest citizen and Bukele a criminal, and Radio Télé Confiance names him as Robentz Dorvil, a 2024 prison escapee. Erik Prince calls the strike a joint Salvadoran and police task force effort. 11 Nov: Bukele posts video of helicopters firing on Lanmò San Jou's house and says most members "ran off without even trying to fight"; Le Repère says nearly 20 were killed and that he was last seen in Carrefour. A suspected member known as "Lisifè" was lynched in Arcahaie on 10 Nov, where he had fled (Radio Télé Confiance; not yet reported by others). Rolfy Martínez reposts an older video of Lanmò San Jou saying he cannot be caught and would go to the United States. 10 Nov: the Miami Herald, citing unnamed sources, says Lanmò San Jou was not home when the Task Force hit his house at about 2:30 a.m. on 8 Nov, that up to 20 gunmen inside were killed, and that the house, with two pools, seems to have been built in the last year. Commentator Rudy Sanon says he is dead (relayed by Rolfy Martínez, who says it has not been established). The Herald says 400 Mawozo reinforced Chen Mechan in the 18 Oct ambush and that Joseph proposed 18 Nov to open talks. 9 Nov: Haitian outlets, citing police sources, say drone strikes destroyed Lanmò San Jou's house in Croix-des-Bouquets at about 3 a.m. on 8 Nov; a photo shows it in ruins. The Jamaica Observer (CMC) and Diario Libre say he survived and is hiding. Radio Télé Aquarelle says he is in Carrefour demanding Chen Mechan's head (not yet reported by others). 8 Nov: a 6 Nov communiqué asks for a pause in offensive operations, cites the Geneva Conventions to protect the house Bukele threatened, and calls a "peaceful" demonstration for 18 Nov in Croix-des-Bouquets (Jemi TV, El Testigo). Drone video shows a white flag on the compound (the gang dates it to 20 Aug). A witness saw Lanmò San Jou leaving a hotel in Thor, Carrefour, on 6 Nov (AyiboPost); RNDDH says Krisla drove him to "Thor 65." The disarmament commission says there is no general amnesty; the UN says receiving his letter is not recognition. 7 Nov: the gang tells AP it contacted Haiti's disarmament commission, plans a peace demonstration, wants a pause in some military operations and an end to "international interference"; an anonymous spokesperson says disarmament is a process. Bukele asks in Creole for the exact address of a house tagged as Lanmò San Jou's to destroy it. Rolfy says the leader is about 12 minutes from the Dominican border. Haiti Defense Observer posts coordinates for another compound. 6 Nov: FJKL reads Lanmò San Jou's letter to Guterres as a bid for legitimacy and asks the UN to use it to end gang impunity; Ulloa says the Cul-de-Sac fight is aimed at Chen Mechan and 400 Mawozo. Rolfy posts a photo he says shows cash bags for the gang. 5 Nov: a man presented as its presumed leader says on video he offered to negotiate and will "attack with everything I have" if Bukele refuses (primiciainftv; not yet reported by others). The police found the burned vehicle of the four Salvadorans at Santo. 4 Nov: the UN says the letter came by email and it is "taking a look," but "it does not imply that there will be a response"; US ambassador Waltz mocked the mediation request. New videos show Lanmò San Jou saying he has no problem with Bukele and dressing up for a feast in Laboule (Rolfy Martínez). 3 Nov: Lanmò San Jou told AP he wrote to Guterres, says he is ready to lay down his weapons, and says the four Salvadorans are dead and were attacked in Chen Mechan territory (no evidence given); the UN said it received the letter but does not recognize him (AP via a search summary). Salvadoran-led armor moved toward his stronghold on 2 Nov (Radio graphie video). 2 Nov: a letter dated 1 Nov in Lanmò San Jou's name, on a 400 Mawozo "Bureau Communication" heading, asks Guterres for UN mediation and a dialogue with the international forces, says he raised a "white flag" on 20 Aug, and denounces Salvadoran operations in the Cul-de-Sac plain (Rezo Nodwes, GJB NewsHT); Serge Pierre Louis says the document alone proves neither who wrote it nor that the UN got it. In a video (Tendencias, 1 Nov) he asks to make peace with Bukele and denies a role in the four's disappearance. Another video shows him cutting trees under armed guard (Rolfy Martínez). Edwin Paraison says men are deserting his gang (Listín Diario). 1 Nov: in a new video Lanmò San Jou says his grave is ready and that Bukele is close, in Croix-des-Bouquets (@kapulet); he also showed new long guns (Parrain Infos asks whether they came from the Dominican Republic). Six members reportedly surrendered at Clercine (Parrain Infos, citing Guerrier Henry). HaitiInfoProj says the gangs massing on Arcahaie attacked "for Bawon Laplen." Le Quotidien 509 notes the UN Panel of Experts found ammunition marked "ERD" (Dominican Army) with the gang. 31 Oct: new videos from Lanmò San Jou mock Bukele's prisons and speak of peace while saying he fears no one; another shows a unit Rolfy Martínez calls "Unité Bawon Laplen" (Rolfy Martínez). Radio Télé Confiance says its base is 12 km from the Dominican border. With Chen Mechan it blocked the roads of Rémy (HaitiInfoProj). 29 Oct: new videos show Lanmò San Jou visiting a church (Rolfy Martínez); AI-made TikTok videos imitating him are spreading (HaitiInfoProj). Led by Wilson Joseph, "Lanmò San Jou," operating in Croix-des-Bouquets and part of Viv Ansanm. He posted a video of ammunition and challenged Bukele (Anadolu). On 24 Oct new video showed him in a pickup with ammunition saying "We have to fight just to stay alive" (via HaitiInfoProj). On 25 Oct a TikTok video blamed Chen Mechan for the four Salvadorans' deaths and said he would have taken them alive and negotiated with Bukele; a longer version shows three men he says are police held in his area (Le Patrimoine 509) and, in César Moreno's Spanish version, a call to shoot foreigners. Radio Télé Confiance says he and Jeff Gwo Lwa have been in Saut-d'Eau since Wednesday. Listín Diario, citing the UN Panel of Experts, says most of 900,000 rounds diverted from Dominican police stocks went to 400 Mawozo and other groups. El Demócrata reports fighting against the gang in Croix-des-Bouquets. On 26 Oct he posted video singing and showing diamond teeth (Rolfy Martínez), and he says in a video that he holds three Haitian police officers; Herald sources say 400 Mawozo backed Chen Mechan's gunmen in the 18 Oct attack. His 25 Oct TikTok: https://www.tiktok.com/@plip_plip_400/video/7700668419598732558. On 27 Oct he posted video of himself walking in Port-au-Prince to a song about the four Salvadorans, and an AI-made video telling Bukele that God will not protect his soldiers "because they have vodou" (Rolfy Martínez). Reuters says he denies holding the men. On 28 and 29 Oct Rolfy Martínez posted videos from the gang's own social media: Lanmò San Jou in what Martínez calls his former mansion, handing out cash from a backpack, and showing off a pet crocodile. Accounts without named sources say his house in Croix-des-Bouquets was destroyed and that he fled to Port-au-Prince; one says he called the soldiers' deaths a mistake and asked Bukele to leave (LbaNews). source

Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W) · Port-au-Prince (18.547°N, 72.340°W) (city center) · Carrefour (18.535°N, 72.409°W) · Thomazeau (18.652°N, 72.094°W) · Arcahaie (18.770°N, 72.512°W) · Laboule (18.503°N, 72.311°W) (neighborhood, approximate point)
Chen Mechan ("Vicious Dog")

12 Nov: Radio Télé Confiance says Chen Mechan and Lanmò San Jou have taken refuge in Carrefour and now "beg the United Nations" for dialogue. 10 Nov: the Miami Herald says several sources blame the 18 Oct ambush of the four Salvadorans on Chen Mechan, with 400 Mawozo reinforcements. 9 Nov: Radio Télé Aquarelle says Lanmò San Jou blames Chen Mechan for the trouble with Bukele and that gang chiefs meeting in Carrefour are discussing his fate (no source named; not yet reported by others). 8 Nov: RNDDH (via FCN) says Claudy Célestin, "Chen Mechan," and Lanmò San Jou have left their usual positions under the Cul-de-Sac and Croix-des-Missions operations, and members have moved to other areas. 7 Nov: no fresh ground item in this run beyond the wider Cul-de-Sac pressure already on the page; Infobae still names the group with 400 Mawozo as a main Cul-de-Sac power. 6 Nov: Ulloa (via Infobae) names Chen Mechan and 400 Mawozo as the main targets of the Cul-de-Sac operations tied to the four's disappearance. 5 Nov: the burned vehicle of the four Salvadorans was found at Santo (police communiqué); Zanmi Lasante reports damage at its Santo site. 4 Nov: El Salvador Times says the troops found its Santo base empty; En el Foco says 14 hostages were freed there (no source). The Miami Herald names its leader as Claudy Célestin (alias Stevenson Pierre). Gangs attacked Chadian troops of the force in Croix-des-Bouquets on 3 Nov and were driven off by Salvadoran helicopters (Bukele). 2 Nov: the first big Salvadoran and police operation hit its stronghold at Santo and Croix-des-Missions; Haitian outlets report about ten members killed, some by kamikaze drones, from unnamed sources (PouSiOuPaTKonnen, Radio graphie, Amerique.info7). 2 Nov: Edwin Paraison, a former Haitian consul, says men are deserting Claudy Célestin's gang too (Listín Diario). 1 Nov: Parrain Infos says a Salvadoran commando reached the Santo house of Claudy Célestin, whom the 400 Mawozo leader had blamed for seizing the Salvadorans, but he escaped. Residents of Canaan marched against the gangs and pointed out where they operate (TVGT Noticias video, citing La Capital News). 31 Oct: Radio Télé Confiance reports on how its leader forces children to carry weapons; with 400 Mawozo it blocked the roads of Rémy with rocks (HaitiInfoProj). Theti Mapping says Salvadoran operations target it and 400 Mawozo in Croix-des-Bouquets and Santo. 30 Oct: a Santo resident, not named, tells Radio Télé Confiance the gang forces youths over 17 to join, threatens those who refuse with death and charges up to 1,500 gourdes to leave, with checkpoints from Santo 9 to Santo 17 and Chada. Part of Viv Ansanm. Haïti Liberté, relaying Chérizier, says its fighters ambushed the GSF members in Santo; Lanmò San Jou now says the four died in its area. Salazar names Claude Celestin as its head. Martínez says its base was destroyed (not yet reported by others). The burned remains handed over on 24 Oct were collected at a drop site in its territory (Miami Herald). Radio Télé Confiance says its leader is dug in at Marin 12; another source, Thériel Thélus, says he is hiding in Lizon and moving by motorcycle, that the gang has regrouped and blocked the Croix-des-Missions bridge, and that a video attributed to it says the Salvadorans fired first and that its men tried to put out the fire (not yet reported by others). On 26 Oct César Moreno, citing one local, named former deputy Lemaire Pierre as the man arming the gang to control 34 neighborhoods (not yet reported by others); police and GSF offensives were reported in Marin and Lizon. On 27 Oct César Moreno, citing unnamed people on the ground, said its leader was surrounded in Marin 21 and his partner, Nicole, killed (not yet reported by others). Accounts without named sources say its leader's house in Croix-des-Bouquets was destroyed on 28 Oct and that nothing is known of where Claudy Célestin is (@HazimNoelia). source

Map: Croix-des-Bouquets (18.578°N, 72.230°W) · Carrefour (18.535°N, 72.409°W)
"Taliban" gang (Cabaret and Kenscoff fighters)

11 Nov: Radio Métronome says three boats bringing reinforcements to the gang near Montrouis were hit by helicopters; the Arcahaie mayor (Le Nouvelliste) says two police helicopters stopped them on 7 Nov and that the latest attacks killed 24 people. 4 Nov: Centre à la Une says suspected "Taliban" members were killed at Vigner and Pon Toman on 3 Nov. Named by Vant Bèf Info (Uguenson Auguste) as the gang that attacked Arcahaie on 31 Oct, reinforced by men from Kenscoff; dead, wounded and burned houses reported. source

Map: Montrouis (18.950°N, 72.705°W) · Kenscoff (18.450°N, 72.287°W) · Arcahaie (18.770°N, 72.512°W) · Cabaret (18.734°N, 72.418°W)
Galaxie's gang (Cabaret, Bési/Bercy)

11 Nov: the Arcahaie mayor asks for a police operation to drive gunmen out of Trou Forban and Bercy. 5 Nov: the Arcahaie mayor puts the 31 Oct to 2 Nov attacks at 16 dead (Le Nouvelliste); Vant Bèf Info calls them revenge for Galaxy's killing by police on 29 Oct and attributes them to Viv Ansanm. 4 Nov: the Arcahaie mayor says at least 22 hostages were freed at Bercy on 2 Nov and some people were burned in their houses; police and brigades retook the road from Thomas to Bercy (Radio Télé Confiance). 3 Nov: Magazine Haïti says more than 20 gang members fell in fighting with the police at Bèsi on 2 Nov. 2 Nov: the counterattack on Arcahaie went on through 1 Nov; a local toll gives 43 dead, 13 of them civilians (Rezo Nodwes), another more than 30 (Centre à la Une). Radio Métronome says the attackers retook Bercy, Vignier and Sourigole, came from Cabaret with help from Kenscoff and the Cul-de-Sac plain, and are tied to Izo 5 Segond and Jeff Gwo Lwa (Montrouis); the police call Bercy a "Taliban" gang position and say about 20 were killed there on 29 Oct (InnovaNews7). 31 Oct: men from Cabaret, reinforced from Kenscoff, struck back at Arcahaie (Vigner, Thoman, Sou Rigòl, Bercy); Amerique.info7 (via Descargador Miniaturas) calls it revenge for Galaxie and says 12 civilians were killed; police and the brigade pushed them back overnight. Its leader, "Galaxie," said to be from Matla, was killed with about ten men (15 per Rolfy Martínez) on 29 Oct at Bercy, between Cabaret and Arcahaie, by the police backed by the Task Force (Le Nouvelliste), with kamikaze drones according to the Arcahaie mayor; others fled toward Lévêque. source

Map: Montrouis (18.950°N, 72.705°W) · Kenscoff (18.450°N, 72.287°W) · Arcahaie (18.770°N, 72.512°W) · Cabaret (18.734°N, 72.418°W)
Bel-Air gang

12 Nov: BINUH says gangs returned to Bel-Air, Bas Delmas and La Saline from July to September after being driven out early in 2026. 9 Nov: Innova News, citing a police source, says police and the Task Force fired at least four drones at kidnapping hideouts in Bel-Air, Delmas 2, Rue Saint-Martin and Ruelle Mayard on 8 Nov; Le Relief says three, with panic in Solino. Downtown Port-au-Prince; Radio Télé Confiance calls Bel-Air a hotbed of kidnappings. Hit by police drone strikes on 25 Oct. A resident says its second in command, "Solo," under "Pèse," a soldier for Izo, was shot (HaitiInfoProj; not yet reported by others). source

Map: Port-au-Prince (18.547°N, 72.340°W) (city center) · Bel-Air (18.550°N, 72.336°W) (neighborhood, approximate point) · Delmas (18.552°N, 72.292°W) · Solino (18.551°N, 72.319°W) (neighborhood, approximate point)
Kraze Baryè

Controls territory in Tabarre where a GSF patrol came under fire in July (Haitian Times). source

Map: Tabarre (18.562°N, 72.251°W)
Gran Grif, Kokorat San Ras and Canaan

12 Nov: Canaan leader Jeff Larose ("Jeff Gwo Lwa") asks Haitians for forgiveness in a video relayed by Rolfy Martínez and says he is giving up attacks in Canaan and Mirebalais (not yet reported by others). BINUH says task force drones struck Kokorat San Ras and Gran Grif in Dessalines, L'Estère and Petite-Rivière from July to September. 10 Nov: KILBA, in a 3 Nov letter to the UN Secretary-General (Radio Métronome), says Gran Grif and Kokorat San Ras and other armed groups hold seven of the ten Lower Artibonite communes, naming Pont-Sondé, Liancourt, Jean-Denis, Petite-Rivière, Marchand-Dessalines, L'Estère and Gros-Morne, and asks for Salvadoran troops there. 9 Nov: Kokorat San Ras freed ONAPE director Stéphanie Paultre on 7 Nov after two months; Vant Bèf Info says more than $400,000 was paid, Satellite509 says the sum was not disclosed. Lawyer Caleb Jean-Baptiste is still held. Gran Grif (led by Luckson Élan) has pressed an offensive on Petite Rivière since 19 Oct (Miami Herald); IOM counts 13,912 people displaced there and in Verrettes. On 29 Oct a video showed a man presented as its leader challenging the Salvadorans and the force in Gonaïves (@vargavila). From March 2026 they attacked dozens of localities in the lower Artibonite; Gran Grif was designated a terrorist group by the US with Viv Ansanm (UN Panel of Experts report, hosted on HaitiDocs, and State Department). source

Map: Artibonite (19.337°N, 72.492°W) (department, approximate center) · Mirebalais (18.834°N, 72.105°W) · Pont-Sondé (19.149°N, 72.616°W) · Dessalines (19.261°N, 72.517°W) · Verrettes (19.051°N, 72.467°W) · Liancourt (19.126°N, 72.541°W) · Gonaïves (19.446°N, 72.688°W)
Boston gang (Cité Soleil)

9 Nov: the police communiqué says Mathias was shot on 5 Nov near the Aciérie d'Haïti and died on 6 Nov. Tag News says Viv Ansanm named "Donald, alias Insikoni," of 3rd Site Lumière as new leader ("remains to be officially confirmed"); another source says Chemali and Baky. 8 Nov: RNDDH says the drone hit Mathias's home and vehicle at Nan Nélio between 9 and 10 p.m. on 5 Nov; he died about 5:30 p.m. on 6 Nov in a hospital in the Canaan area; police say he was shot. Two names, "Chemali" and "Baky," circulate as successors (Etienne Côté-Paluck, one local collaborator). An AyiboPost UN source calls him the number two of the G9 alliance; Latinar calls the gang a neighborhood group of about 100; Descifrando la Guerra says it was a brake on the 400 Mawozo–Chen Mechan–Canaan–Tokyo bloc. 7 Nov: Mathias Saintil (also Sainthil / Saint-Vil), head of the Boston gang in Cité Soleil and Carrefour Drouillard, dies after a 5 Nov police and Task Force kamikaze-drone strike on his vehicle; a helicopter also fired, per Radio Télé Confiance. UN (via DW) had put the group at about 100 members in 2024, accused of killings, arson, rape and robbery. First gang chief killed in a Haitian drone operation (DW / EFE / Reuters). Prime Minister Fils-Aimé posts "one gang chief less" without naming him. source

Map: Cité Soleil (18.578°N, 72.335°W) · Carrefour (18.535°N, 72.409°W)
Brooklyn gang (Cité Soleil)

8 Nov: RNDDH says a pickup with fifteen armed men of Ti Gabriel, the Brooklyn leader, heading to reinforce Mathias was stopped by the task force on 5 Nov: five killed, six wounded (Miami Herald, FCN). Radio Télé Confiance says, without naming him, that the party in power's candidate put a Brooklyn gang leader at the head of the interim gang alliance in Cité Soleil and sent two million gourdes through a former deputy to "Ti Gabriel" for the 1 Nov Day of the Dead (28 Oct; not yet reported by others). source

Map: Cité Soleil (18.578°N, 72.335°W)
Krisla

13 Nov: Innova News says Carrefour, held by Viv Ansanm gangs, is the next target after Village-de-Dieu and Martissant. 12 Nov: Radio Télé Confiance says gang chiefs who fled the northern suburbs, including Chen Mechan and Lanmò San Jou, are in Krisla's Carrefour. 11 Nov: Radio Télé Confiance says Krisla's gunmen have put up barricades and armed vehicle checks on Carrefour's roads, where gang members fleeing other areas are gathering (not yet reported by others). Chérizier, in a video, accuses the prime minister of giving Krisla weapons and says both should stand trial, offering no evidence. 9 Nov: Radio Télé Aquarelle says many Viv Ansanm chiefs have taken refuge at Chrisla's base in Carrefour (not yet reported by others). 8 Nov: RNDDH (via the Miami Herald) names Chrisla "Krisla" Chéry as leader of the Ti Bwa gang and says he drove Lanmò San Jou to Thor 65, Carrefour; AyiboPost says gang leaders including Krisla met at a Thor hotel on 5 Nov and that a father there was extorted by him. Named by the Miami Herald's analyst as one of two leaders who spoke publicly this week. source

Map: Village de Dieu (18.538°N, 72.350°W) (neighborhood, approximate point) · Martissant (18.527°N, 72.357°W) (neighborhood, approximate point) · Carrefour (18.535°N, 72.409°W)
Izo 5 Segond (Village de Dieu)

13 Nov: Haitian and Salvadoran soldiers are positioned at Portail Léogâne by the Sylvio Cator stadium, next to Village de Dieu (Haiti Defense Observer, AP photos); Innova News says reconnaissance is under way before operations there. 12 Nov: a commentator relayed by Rolfy Martínez says Izo wants to be sent to a prison in Colombia (no evidence; not yet reported by others). BINUH says task force drones targeted Village de Dieu and Grand Ravine downtown from July to September. 9 Nov: Centre à la Une says one of two men arrested on National Road 3 near Colladère is linked to Izo 5 Segond, from data on his phone. 8 Nov: late on 7 Nov, heavy gunfire near the downtown flour mill and "some panic in Village de Dieu," with a drone blast downtown (Tag News via HaitiInfoProj; not yet reported by others). 4 Nov: HaitiInfoProj posted a photo of "5 Segond" men from the Bercy base climbing walls in Arcahaie before police and residents drove them back. Led by former police officer Johnson André (Izo). 2 Nov: Rolfy Martínez says "it is said" his second in command was killed in clashes with the joint forces and police in Village de Dieu (no source named). Radio Métronome ties the Arcahaie attackers to it. source

Map: Village de Dieu (18.538°N, 72.350°W) (neighborhood, approximate point) · Arcahaie (18.770°N, 72.512°W) · Léogâne (18.511°N, 72.633°W)
